Al-Ṭabarī states the worthiest of the sayings: the correctness of the saying, with us, of the wisdom, is that it is the knowledge of the rulings of God, whose knowledge is not reached except by the making plain of the Messenger ﷺ, and the acquaintance with it, and what its likes indicate of it; and it is, with me, taken from the ḥukm, in the meaning of the separating between the truth and the falsehood. The sūrah of al-Jumuʿah states the same raising, in the same wording: (62:2 — **“He is the One Who raised for the illiterate ˹people˺ a messenger from among themselves”**). The supplication of the verse stands, then, as the bridge between the building of the House and the sending of the Messenger: the two builders asked the reciting, and the teaching, and the purifying — and the answer of the supplication is the Messenger who does the three, among the offspring the two asked for.

---

## Verse 2:130

> And who would reject the faith of Abraham except a fool! We certainly chose him in this life, and in the Hereafter he will surely be among the righteous

**THE QUESTION THAT IS A NEGATION**

The passage of Ibrāhīm closes with the question that answers the two houses. Al-Ṭabarī reads: God means, by His saying, and who turns away from the faith of Ibrāhīm — who is it that renounces the faith of Ibrāhīm, and leaves it, in turning away from it, to other than it? And God intended, by that, the Jews and the Christians, for their choosing, of what they chose of the Judaism and the Christianity, over the Islam — because the faith of Ibrāhīm is the ḥanīfiyyah muslimah, as God said of him (3:67 — **“Abraham was neither a Jew nor a Christian”**); so God said to them: who turns away from the faith of Ibrāhīm, the ḥanīfiyyah muslimah, except the one who is a fool in his self? Qatādah said: the Jews and the Christians turned away from his faith, and they took the Judaism and the Christianity, an innovation not from God, and they left the faith of Ibrāhīm — meaning, the Islam; likewise, God sent His Prophet Muḥammad ﷺ with the faith of Ibrāhīm. Al-Rabīʿ said: the Jews and the Christians turned away from the faith of Ibrāhīm, and they innovated the Judaism and the Christianity, and it is not from God, and they left the faith of Ibrāhīm: the Islam. Al-Qurṭubī reads the form of the question: who is an interrogative, in the place of the raising, by the subject, and turns away is its connector, and except the one who is a fool in his self is in the place of the predicate; and it is a rebuke and a reproach, in which is the meaning of the negation — meaning, none turns away, al-Naḥḥās said it.

**THE FOOL IN HIS SELF**

The fooling of the verse is given its grammar and its meaning. Al-Ṭabarī reads: God means, by His saying, except the one who is a fool in his self — except the one whose self is a fool; and the meaning of the speech is: none turns away from the faith of Ibrāhīm, the ḥanīf, except a fool, ignorant of the place of the fortune of his self, in what benefits it and harms it, in its return; and the self is installed upon the meaning of the explicating — because the fooling, in the origin, is of the self, and when it was transferred to who, the self was installed, by the meaning of the explicating; as it is said, he is the widest of you, in house — the house enters the speech, because the wideness is in it, not in the man; and likewise, the self was entered, because the fooling is of the self, not of who.

**THE CHOOSING AND THE RIGHTEOUS**

The verse closes with the two standings of Ibrāhīm: the choosing in the world, and the righteousness in the Hereafter. Al-Ṭabarī reads the choosing by its consequence: this is a report from God that whoever opposes Ibrāhīm, in what he set for the ones after him, opposes God, and an informing, of Him to His creation, that whoever opposes what Muḥammad ﷺ came with opposes Ibrāhīm — because God reported that He chose him for His intimacy, and made him, for the people, an imām, and reported that his religion was the ḥanīfiyyah muslimah; and in that is the plainest making plain, from God, that whoever opposes him is an enemy of God, for his opposing the imām God set up for His servants. And he reads the last of the verse: and he, in the Hereafter, is of the righteous — of the ones whose righteousness is accepted, and whose standing is with the righteous of the servants of God. The Book states the following of that faith, to the Messenger who came with it: (16:123 — **“Follow the faith of Abraham, the upright, who was not one of the polytheists”**). The passage of Ibrāhīm closes, then, upon the measure of the whole sūrah to come: the faith that is the ḥanīfiyyah muslimah, the imām who is the father of the prophets, and the turning away from it that is the fooling of the self — and the houses that claim the Garden are answered, at the close of the passage, by the father they all claim, and the faith they all left.

---

## Verse 2:131

> When his Lord ordered him, “Submit ˹to My Will˺,” he responded, “I submit to the Lord of all worlds.”

**THE COMMAND AND ITS ANSWER**

The verse gives the imāmate of Ibrāhīm its moment: the command, and the answer. Al-Ṭabarī reads: God means, by His saying, when his Lord said to him, submit — when his Lord said to him: make sincere for Me the worship, and submit to Me in the obedience; and the meaning of His saying, he said, I submit to the Lord of the worlds, is: Ibrāhīm said, answering his Lord: I submitted in the obedience, and made sincere the worship, to the Owner of all the creation and its Disposer, without other than Him. The command of the verse is thus the command of the Islam that the sūrah has been naming since its beginning: the sincerity of the worship, and the submitting of the heart and the limbs; and the answer is given in the same root — aslamtu — as if the servant's word does no more than return to him the word of his Lord.

**THE TIME OF THE COMMAND**

Al-Ṭabarī raises the question of the time, and answers it: if one says — I have known that idh is a time, so what is it timed by, and what is it attached to? It is said: it is attached to His saying, and We chose him, in the world; and the interpretation of the speech is: and We chose him, in the world, when his Lord said to him, submit; he said, I submit to the Lord of the worlds; and the meaning of the speech is only: and We chose him, in the world, when We said to him, submit; and He made plain the name of God, in His saying, when his Lord said to him, submit, upon the face of the report of one absent, though His mention had run before, upon the face of the report of Himself — as Khufāf ibn Nudbah said: I say to him, while the spear bends his back: consider Khufāf, I am that one.

The questioner then asks: did God call Ibrāhīm to the Islam? It is said: yes, He called him to it. And he says: in what state did He call him to it? It is said: when he said, O my people, I am innocent of what you associate; I have turned my face to the One who originated the heavens and the earth, a ḥanīf, and I am not of the polytheists — and that is the time his Lord said to him, submit: from after He tried him, by the stars, and the moon, and the sun. The sūrah of the Cattle states the turning of that time: (6:79 — **“I have turned my face towards the One Who has originated the heavens and the earth—being upright”**). The choosing of the verse before is timed, then, by the submitting: Ibrāhīm was chosen in the world, at the hour he was told to submit, and he submitted — and the hour of the answer is the hour of the imāmate.

---

## Verse 2:132

> This was the advice of Abraham—as well as Jacob—to his children, ˹saying˺, “Indeed, Allah has chosen for you this faith; so do not die except in ˹a state of full˺ submission.”

**THE WORD THAT WAS BEQUEATHED**

The verse carries the word of the submitting from the father to the sons. Al-Ṭabarī reads: God means, by His saying, and Ibrāhīm charged with it — he charged, with this word; what is intended by the word is His saying, I submit to the Lord of the worlds, and it is the Islam with which He commanded His Prophet ﷺ — and it is the sincerity of the worship and the singling of God, and the submitting of the heart and the limbs to Him; and by His saying, and he charged his sons with it, He means: he covenanted with them, by that, and commanded them with it. Qatādah said of His saying, and Ibrāhīm charged his sons with it, and Yaʿqūb: he means, Yaʿqūb charged his sons with it, after Ibrāhīm. Ibn ʿAbbās said: he charged them with the Islam, and Yaʿqūb charged with the like of that. And a group of the reciters read: and Ibrāhīm enjoined with it, in the meaning of the covenant.

The bequest is read by its two sayings. Some said: His saying, and Ibrāhīm charged his sons with it, is a report completed, and His saying, and Yaʿqūb, is a begun report — Ibrāhīm charged his sons, that they say, we submit to the Lord of the worlds; and Yaʿqūb charged his sons: O my sons, God has chosen for you the religion, so do not die except as Muslims. Al-Ṭabarī refuses: there is no meaning to the saying of the one who said that — because what Yaʿqūb charged his sons with is the like of what Ibrāhīm charged his sons with, of the urging upon the obedience of God, and the submitting to Him, and the Islam.

**THE AN THAT WAS DROPPED**

The verse is given its grammar by the dropping of the word. Al-Ṭabarī raises the question: if the matter is as you described — that its meaning is, and Ibrāhīm charged his sons with it, and Yaʿqūb: that, O my sons — then what is the reason the an is dropped from the speech? It is said: because the bequest is a saying, and it was carried upon its meaning — for if that had come in the wording of the saying, the an would not have been good with it, and it would only have been said: and Ibrāhīm said to his sons, and Yaʿqūb: O my sons; so when the bequest was a saying, it was carried upon its meaning, without its wording, and the an that is good with it was dropped — as God said, God enjoins you, concerning your children, for the male, the like of the share of the two females; and as the poet said: I will show to you, in what I show — I have two longings, a longing for Najd, and a longing for me, in the land of Sindh — the an was dropped, when the showing, by the tongue, was a saying in meaning, and he carried it upon its meaning, without its wording. The word of the verse is thus carried from father to son, in the form of the bequest that is the form of the saying — and the grammar itself records that the bequest of the faith is of the speech, between the one who gives it and the ones who receive it.

**THE DEATH IN THE SUBMISSION**

The content of the bequest is given its form: God has chosen for you the religion, so do not die except as Muslims. The choosing is ascribed to God, and the dying is addressed to the sons — and the bequest is not the command of the choosing, but the command of the standing in it: the religion is chosen for them, and they are asked not to die except upon it. The sūrah of Āl ʿImrān carries the same command, to the community of the Messenger ﷺ: (3:102 — **“do not die except in ˹a state of full˺ submission”**). The bequest of the fathers is thus the same bequest the Book gives the sons of the last time — and the Islam that was the word of Ibrāhīm, at his command, is the word of the bequest, at his death, and the word of the community, until the Day.

---

## Verse 2:133

> Or did you witness when death came to Jacob? He asked his children, “Who will you worship after my passing?” They replied, “We will ˹continue to˺ worship your God, the God of your forefathers—Abraham, Ishmael, and Isaac—the One God. And to Him we ˹all˺ submit.”

**THE QUESTION OF THE DEATHBED**

The verse brings the scene of the bequest to its deathbed, and it brings the two houses to witness it. Al-Ṭabarī reads the standing of the question: were you — O assembly of the Jews and the Christians, who call Muḥammad ﷺ a liar, who deny his prophethood — present, and witnesses, when the death attended Yaʿqūb? Meaning, you were not present at that — so do not claim, upon My prophets and My messengers, the falsehoods, and do not ascribe to them the Judaism and the Christianity; for I sent My intimate friend Ibrāhīm — and his sons Isḥāq and Ismāʿīl, and their offspring — with the ḥanīfiyyah muslimah, and by that they charged their sons, and by it they covenanted with their children after them; and if you had been present with them, and heard from them, you would have known that they were upon other than the religions and the faiths you ascribed to them, after them. Al-Rabīʿ said of His saying, or were you witnesses: He means the People of the Book. And these verses were sent down, a declaring false, from God, of the Jews and the Christians, in their claim, upon Ibrāhīm and his son Yaʿqūb, that they were upon their faith.

**THE ANSWER OF THE SONS**

The answer of the sons is read by its two faces. Al-Ṭabarī reads: God means, by His saying, what will you worship, after me — what thing will you worship, after me? — meaning, after my death; they said, we worship your God — his sons said to him: we worship your worshipped One, whom you worship, and the worshipped One of your fathers Ibrāhīm and Ismāʿīl and Isḥāq, one God — meaning, we make sincere for Him the worship, and single Him in the Lordship, and associate nothing with Him, and take no lord besides Him. And his saying, and we are Muslims to Him, can bear the meaning of the state — as if they said, we worship your God, Muslims to Him, by our obedience and our worship of Him; and it can bear a begun report — we worship your God, after you, and we are Muslims to Him, now, and in every state; and the better of the two faces is that it be in the meaning of the state: we worship your God, and the God of your fathers Ibrāhīm and Ismāʿīl and Isḥāq, Muslims to His worship.

**THE UNCLE WHO IS COUNTED A FATHER**

The verse carries the question of the genealogy. Some of the predecessors read: and the God of your father, Ibrāhīm — supposing that Ismāʿīl, since he was an uncle to Yaʿqūb, was not permissible to be among the ones translated by the fathers, and entered in their number; and that is, from its reader so, a lack of knowledge of the courses of the speech of the Arabs — for the Arabs do not refrain from making the uncles in the meaning of the fathers, and the maternal uncles in the meaning of the mothers; and for that reason Ismāʿīl entered among the ones translated by the fathers. And the correctness of the reading, with us, is, and the God of your fathers, for the agreement of the reciters upon the correcting of that, and the singling out of the one who opposed them of the reciters. The sūrah of Yūsuf gives the answer of the sons its echo, three generations after: (12:38 — **“I follow the faith of my fathers: Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob”**). The deathbed of the verse is thus the standing in which the religion is asked of the sons — and the answer of the sons of Yaʿqūb is the answer of Yūsuf after them, and the answer of the community after Yūsuf: the God of the fathers, one, and the Islam to Him.

---

## Verse 2:134

> That was a community that had already gone before. For them is what they earned and for you is what you have earned. And you will not be accountable for what they have done

**THE COMMUNITY THAT HAS GONE BEFORE**

The verse closes the passage of the fathers with the summing of the standing. Al-Ṭabarī names the community: God means, by His saying, that is a community that has passed: Ibrāhīm, and Ismāʿīl, and Isḥāq, and Yaʿqūb, and their offspring. And He says to the Jews and the Christians: O assembly of the Jews and the Christians, leave the mention of Ibrāhīm, and Ismāʿīl, and Isḥāq, and Yaʿqūb, and the Muslims of their offspring, with other than what they are its people, and do not ascribe to them the disbelief of the Judaism and the Christianity, adding it to them; for they are a community — and He means, by the community, in this place, the group and the generation of the people — that has passed: it has gone its way. Al-Qurṭubī reads the word: that is a subject, and a community is its predicate, and has passed is an attribute of the community; and if you wish, it is the predicate of the subject, and a community is a substitute of that. Al-Ṭabarī gives the passing its root in the tongue: it is only said of the one who has died and gone, he has passed, for his vacating of the world, and his being alone, from what was of the familiarity of his people and his companions, in his world; and its root is from their saying, the man passed, when he came to the place in which is no one to familiarize him, and was alone from the people; and that was used of the one who dies, upon that face.

**THE EARNING OF EACH**

The verse then divides the earning between the two. Al-Ṭabarī reads: God said, then, to the Jews and the Christians: to the one to whom you ascribed — your misguidance and your disbelief, which you are upon — of My prophets and My messengers, is what he earned; and for you, O assembly of the Jews and the Christians, is the like of that, what you worked; and you are not taken, you — the ones who ascribed what you ascribed to them, of the faiths — and asked of what Ibrāhīm, and Ismāʿīl, and Isḥāq, and Yaʿqūb, and their offspring used to work, and earn, of good and evil — for every soul has what it earned, and upon it what it acquired; so leave the ascribing of them, and the ascribing of their faiths; for the claims do not avail you, with God, and what avails you, with Him, is only what has passed for you, of the righteous of your deeds, if you worked them and sent them forward. Al-Qurṭubī reads the closing: and you are not asked of what they used to work — meaning, no one is taken by the sin of another, the like of His saying, and no bearer bears the burden of another. The sūrah of the Creator states the same law: (35:18 — **“No soul burdened with sin will bear the burden of another”**). The verse is thus the boundary between the community that passed and the ones who claim it: the fathers have their earning, and the sons have theirs — and the ascribing of the faith of the fathers is not an earning, and the claim of the names is not a deed.

---

## Verse 2:135

> The Jews and Christians each say, “Follow our faith to be ˹rightly˺ guided.” Say, ˹O Prophet,˺ “No! We follow the faith of Abraham, the upright—who was not a polytheist.”

**THE TWO CALLS**

Ibn Isḥāq carries the verse its occasion, from Ibn ʿAbbās: ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣūriyā al-Aʿwar said to the Messenger of God ﷺ: the guidance is only what we are upon! Follow us, O Muḥammad, and you will be guided! And the Christians said the like of that. And God sent down, concerning them: and they say, be Jews or Christians, you will be guided; say: rather, the faith of Ibrāhīm, a ḥanīf, and he was not of the polytheists. Al-Ṭabarī reads the two calls: the Jews said, to Muḥammad ﷺ and his Companions of the believers: be Jews, you will be guided; and the Christians said to them: be Christians, you will be guided.

**THE ANSWER OF THE MILLAH**

Al-Ṭabarī gives the answer its form: God argued, for His Prophet Muḥammad ﷺ, the most eloquent of arguments, and its most concise and complete, and taught it to His Prophet ﷺ, and He said: O Muḥammad, say to the ones who say to you, of the Jews and the Christians, and to your Companions: be Jews or Christians, you will be guided — rather, come, we follow the faith of Ibrāhīm, which all of us agree upon the testifying to it, that it is the religion of God that He was pleased with and chose and commanded with — for his religion was the ḥanīfiyyah muslimah — and we leave the rest of the faiths, in which we differ, and some of us deny them, and some of us acknowledge them; for that, in its difference, is not a way for us to the agreement upon it, as we have the way to the agreement upon the faith of Ibrāhīm. The grammar of the answer is read by its faces: in the installing of His saying, rather, the faith of Ibrāhīm, are three faces — one of them, that the meaning of their saying, be Jews or Christians, be directed to the meaning of, they said, follow the Judaism and the Christianity, and the faith be joined upon that meaning; and it is possible that it be installed upon the face of the urging, to the following of the faith of Ibrāhīm; and some of the reciters read it with the raising — rather, the guidance is the faith of Ibrāhīm.

**THE ḤANĪF, BY ITS FACES**

The ḥanīf of the verse is read by its sayings. Ibn ʿAbbās said, in the report of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭalḥah: a ḥanīf — a pilgrim. Kathīr Abū Sahl said: I asked al-Ḥasan of the ḥanīfiyyah, and he said: the ḥajj of the House; and in his other report: he is the ḥajj of this House. ʿAṭiyyah said: the ḥanīf is the pilgrim. Mujāhid said, of ḥunafāʾ: followers; and in the report of al-Suddī from him: pilgrims. Al-Ṭabarī gives the ḥanīfiyyah its test: if the ḥanīfiyyah were the circumcision, it would be obligatory that the Jews be ḥunafāʾ — and God brought them out of that, by His saying, Ibrāhīm was not a Jew nor a Christian, but he was a ḥanīf, a Muslim. The sūrah of the Cattle states the same guidance, upon the tongue of the Messenger ﷺ: (6:161 — **“Surely my Lord has guided me to the Straight Path, a perfect way, the faith of Abraham, the upright”**). The answer of the verse is thus the refusal of the two calls, by the call that precedes them: the guidance is not in the being Jewish nor the being Christian, but in the faith that was before the two, and that the two houses themselves agree to testify to — the faith of the father, the ḥanīf, who was not of the polytheists.

---

## Verse 2:136

> Say, ˹O believers,˺ “We believe in Allah and what has been revealed to us; and what was revealed to Abraham, Ishmael, Isaac, Jacob, and his descendants; and what was given to Moses, Jesus, and other prophets from their Lord. We make no distinction between any of them. And to Allah we all submit.”

**THE DELEGATION AND THE QUESTION OF THE MESSENGERS**

Ibn Isḥāq carries the verse its occasion, from Ibn ʿAbbās: there came to the Messenger of God ﷺ a group of the Jews, among them Abū Yāsir ibn Akhṭab, and Rāfiʿ ibn Abī Rāfiʿ, and ʿĀzār, and Khālid, and Zayd, and Azār ibn Abī Azār, and Ashyaʿ; and they asked him of whom he believed in, of the messengers; and he said: I believe in God, and what was sent down to us, and what was sent down to Ibrāhīm, and Ismāʿīl, and Isḥāq, and Yaʿqūb, and the Asbāṭ, and what Mūsā was given, and ʿĪsā, and what the prophets were given, from their Lord; we do not differentiate between any of them, and we are Muslims to Him. And when he mentioned ʿĪsā, they denied his prophethood, and said: we do not believe in ʿĪsā, and we do not believe in whoever believed in him. And God sent down, concerning them: say, we believe in God, to the end of the verse. Qatādah said: this verse was sent down, a command from God, of the affirming of all His messengers, by the believers.

**THE CONFESSION WITHOUT THE DISTINCTION**

Al-Ṭabarī reads the confession, item by item: and what was sent down to us — He says: we affirmed the Book God sent down to our Prophet Muḥammad ﷺ; and He ascribed the address, by the sending down, to them, since they were its followers, and its commanded and prohibited — and though it was a sending down to the Messenger of God ﷺ, it was in the meaning of the sending down to them, for the meanings they have in it. And His saying, and what Mūsā and ʿĪsā were given, means: we believed in the Torah God gave Mūsā, and in the Gospel God gave ʿĪsā, and in the Books He gave all the prophets, and we acknowledged and affirmed that all of it is truth and guidance and light, from God, and that all whom God mentioned, of His prophets, were upon truth and guidance, affirming one another, upon one path, in the calling to the singling of God, and the working by His obedience; and we do not differentiate between any of them — He says: we do not believe in some of the prophets and disbelieve in some, and free ourselves of some and take some as allies, as the Jews freed themselves of ʿĪsā and Muḥammad, upon the two be the peace, and affirmed other than them of the prophets, and as the Christians freed themselves of Muḥammad ﷺ and affirmed other than him of the prophets; rather, we testify, for all of them, that they were messengers of God. Al-Rabīʿ gives the Asbāṭ their number: the Asbāṭ are Yūsuf and his brothers, the sons of Yaʿqūb, twelve men; and there was born, to every man of them, a nation of the people, and they were named the Asbāṭ.

**THE RULE OF THE REPORTING**

Al-Qurṭubī carries the verse its ruling, of the ḥadīth of the two collections: al-Bukhārī brought out, from Abū Hurayrah: the People of the Book used to read the Torah in the Hebrew, and interpret it in the Arabic, for the people of the Islam; and the Messenger of God ﷺ said: do not believe the People of the Book, and do not deny them; and say, we believe in God, and what was sent down — the verse. And Muḥammad ibn Sīrīn said: if it is said to you, are you a believer? — say, we believe in God, and what was sent down to us, and what was sent down to Ibrāhīm — the verse; and the majority of the predecessors disliked that the man say, I am a believer, truly. The verse is thus the community's confession and its rule together: it is the form of the belief in all the messengers, and the form of the standing with the books of the others — neither the believing of them, nor the denying of them, but the confessing of what came from God, among what they carry. The sūrah of the Spider states the same standing, with the people of the Book: (29:46 — **“Do not argue with the People of the Book unless gracefully”**).

---

## Verse 2:137

> So if they believe in what you believe, then they will indeed be ˹rightly˺ guided. But if they turn away, they are simply opposed ˹to the truth˺. But Allah will spare you their evil. For He is the All-Hearing, All-Knowing

**THE LIKE OF THE BELIEF**

The verse gives the two houses their measure, and the measure is the belief of the believers. Ibn ʿAbbās said, in the report of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭalḥah: God reported that the belief is the firmest handle, and that He accepts no deed except with it, and the Garden is not forbidden except to the one who leaves it. Al-Qurṭubī reads: the address is to Muḥammad ﷺ and his community; the meaning is: if they believe, the like of your belief, and affirm, the like of your affirming, they are guided — the likening fell between the two beliefs; and it was said: the bāʾ is an added confirming. Al-Ṭabarī carries the reading of Ibn ʿAbbās, and answers it: it was reported from Ibn ʿAbbās, in that, a reading the codices of the Muslims came contrary to, and the reciters agreed upon leaving — he said: do not say, if they believe in the like of what you believed in, for there is no like for God; but say, if they believe in what you believed in; and it is as if Ibn ʿAbbās, in this report, if it be sound from him, directed the interpretation of the one who read, if they believe in the like of what you believed in — if they believe in the like of God, and the like of what was sent down to Ibrāhīm and Ismāʿīl; and that, directed to this face, is an association with God without doubt — for there is no like for God, to be believed in or disbelieved; but the interpretation of that is upon other than the meaning he directed its interpretation to — its meaning is only: if they affirm, the like of your affirming, with what you affirmed, of all that We enumerated for you, of the Books of God and His prophets, they are guided; so the likening only fell between the two affirmings and the two acknowledgings, that are the belief of these and the belief of those — as the saying of the speaker: ʿAmr passed by your brother, the like of what I passed by him — meaning, ʿAmr passed by your brother, the like of my passing by him; and the likening only entered, a likening between the two passings, not between ʿAmr and the speaker.

**THE SHIQĀQ AND ITS FULFILLMENT**

The verse then gives the turning away its name. Qatādah said of His saying, they are only in a shiqāq: in a separation. Al-Rabīʿ said: He means, a separation. Ibn Zayd said: the shiqāq is the separation and the warfare; when one is in shiqāq, he has warred, and when he has warred, he has been in shiqāq, and the two are one, in the speech of the Arabs — and he recited, and whoever is in shiqāq with the Messenger. Al-Ṭabarī reads: God means, by His saying, and if they turn away — if these who said to Muḥammad ﷺ and his Companions, be Jews or Christians, turn away and avert, and do not believe, the like of your belief, O believers, in God, and what the prophets came with, and the messengers were sent with, and differentiate between the messengers of God, and between God and His messengers, and affirm some and disbelieve in some — then know, O believers, that they are only in disobedience, and separation, and war, against God and His Messenger and you. The verse then gives the promise of its fulfillment: God will suffice you against them. Al-Ṭabarī reads the fulfillment: God did that to them, immediate, and accomplished His promise, and sufficed His Prophet ﷺ, by His giving him authority over them, until he killed some of them, and exiled some, and humbled some, and disgraced him, by the jizyah and the smallness. (4:115 — **“And whoever defies the Messenger after guidance has become clear to them”**). The verse carries, then, the two standings of the houses with the belief: the believing of its like, that is the guidance, and the turning away, that is the shiqāq — and the sufficiency of God stands behind the two, the Hearing, the Knowing.

---

## Verse 2:138

> This is the ˹natural˺ Way of Allah. And who is better than Allah in ordaining a way? And we worship ˹none but˺ Him

**THE DYE OF THE TWO HOUSES**

The verse answers the two calls of the houses, with the dye. Qatādah carries the occasion of the word: the Jews dye their children Jews, and the Christians dye their children Christians; and the dye of God is the Islam — and there is no dye more beautiful than the Islam, and no purer; and it is the religion of God, He sent Nūḥ with it, and the prophets after him. ʿAṭāʾ carries the dye of the houses to its face: the Jews dyed their sons, opposing the fiṭra. Al-Suddī said of His saying, the dye of God, and who is more beautiful than God, in dye: the religion of God, and who is more beautiful than God, in religion. Abū al-ʿĀliyah said, in the report of al-Rabīʿ: the dye of God — the religion of God; and who is more beautiful than God, in dye — and who is more beautiful than God, in religion. Mujāhid said: the dye of God — the fiṭra of God, upon which He created the people; and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Kathīr said: the dye of God — the religion of God, and who is more beautiful than God, in religion; and he said: it is the fiṭra of God. The dye of the verse is thus read by the two faces that meet: it is the religion, and it is the fiṭra — and the one who directed the dye to the fiṭra, its meaning is: rather, we follow the fiṭra of God, and His faith, upon which He created His creation, and that is the upright religion.

**THE GRAMMAR AND THE WORSHIP**

The installing of the dye is read by its faces: the one who read it installed read it, a returning upon the faith — rather, we follow the faith of Ibrāhīm, the dye of God; and it is possible that it be installed, upon the saying of the ones who said, say, we believe in God, to his saying, and we are Muslims to Him — the dye of God, in the meaning of, we believed this belief; and the belief is, then, the dye of God; and it is possible that it be raised, upon the beginning: it is the dye of God. Al-Ṭabarī reads the worship that closes the verse: His saying, and we are worshippers to Him, is a command from God to His Prophet ﷺ, that he say it to the Jews and the Christians, who said to him, and to the ones who followed him of his Companions: be Jews or Christians; and He said to His Prophet Muḥammad: say: rather, we follow the faith of Ibrāhīm, a ḥanīf, the dye of God, and we are worshippers to Him — meaning, the faith of the ones who submit to God, who humble to Him, in our following the faith of Ibrāhīm, and our holding to him, by that — not arrogant, in the following of His command, and the affirming of His messengers, as the Jews and the Christians were arrogant, and disbelieved in Muḥammad ﷺ, in arrogance, and transgression, and envy. The sūrah of Rūm states the fiṭra the dye is read by: (30:30 — **“the natural Way of Allah which He has instilled in ˹all˺ people”**). The verse is thus the answer of the dye to the two dyeings: the Jews dye their children, and the Christians dye their children, and the dye of God is the fiṭra they were created upon — and the question, who is more beautiful than God, in dye, is the refusal of the two dyeings, by the question itself.

---

## Verse 2:139

> Say, “Would you dispute with us about Allah, while He is our Lord and your Lord? We are accountable for our deeds and you for yours. And we are devoted to Him ˹alone˺

**THE DISPUTE ABOUT GOD**

The verse gives the disputation its name, and its refusal. Mujāhid said of His saying, say, do you dispute with us in God: he means, do you contend with us? Ibn Zayd said the like. Ibn ʿAbbās said: do you argue with us? Al-Ṭabarī gives the verse its standing: this is, from God, a reproach of the Jews, and an arguing, for the people of the belief — by His saying, to the believers of the Companions of Muḥammad: say, O believers, to the Jews and the Christians who said to you, be Jews or Christians, you will be guided: do you dispute with us in God? — He means, by His saying, in God, in the religion of God, that He commanded us to hold Him by; and our Lord and your Lord is one, a just One who does not wrong, and He rewards the servants only for what they earned; and you claim that you are worthier of God than us, by the precedence of your religion, and your book, and your prophet, and we are making sincere for Him the worship, and we have associated nothing with Him, and you have associated, in your worship of Him — some of you worshipped the calf, and some of you worshipped the Messiah; how then are you better than us, and worthier of God than us?

**THE DEEDS OF EACH**

The verse divides the deeds, as the verse of the community divided the earning: ours is what we worked, and yours is what you worked. Al-Ṭabarī reads the devotion that closes the verse: His saying, and we are sincere to Him, means: we are, for God, the ones who make sincere the worship and the obedience, associating nothing with Him, and worshiping none other than Him — as the people of the idols worshiped, with Him, the idols, and the companions of the calf, with Him, the calf. The dispute about God is answered, by the verse, with the Lordship that is common — our Lord and your Lord is one — and with the deeds that are separate — ours is what we worked, and yours is what you worked; and the sincerity closes the verse upon the singling: and we are sincere to Him. The Lordship of the verse is the ground the two stand upon, and the deeds are the standing of each upon it — and the dispute is refused, because the one disputed about is the Lord of the two parties, and the account is with Him alone. The sūrah of Āl ʿImrān gives the community the word that ends the disputes, before they begin: (3:64 — **“Let us come to common terms: that we will worship none but Allah”**). The verse is thus the closing of the disputation, by the standing above it: the Lord of the disputing parties is one, and the sincerity is the deed of the believers, and the account of the deeds is with the One the two parties dispute about.

---

## Verse 2:140

> Do you claim that Abraham, Ishmael, Isaac, Jacob, and his descendants were all Jews or Christians?” Say, “Who is more knowledgeable: you or Allah?” Who does more wrong than those who hide the testimony they received from Allah? And Allah is never unaware of what you do

**THE CLAIM AND THE QUESTION**

The verse brings the claim of the houses to its last standing, and answers it with the question. Al-Ṭabarī reads the argument: this verse too is an arguing, from God, for His Prophet ﷺ, against the Jews and the Christians whose tales God mentioned; and God says to His Prophet Muḥammad ﷺ: say, O Muḥammad, to these Jews and Christians: do you dispute with us in God, and claim that your religion is better than our religion, and that you are upon guidance, and we are upon misguidance, by a proof from God, and call us to your religion? Then bring your proof of that, and we will follow you upon it — or do you say, Ibrāhīm and Ismāʿīl and Isḥāq and Yaʿqūb and the Asbāṭ were Jews or Christians, upon your religion? Then bring, for your claim of what you claimed of that, a proof, and we will affirm you — for God made them imāms who are followed. Al-Qurṭubī reads the question: say, are you more knowing, or God? — it is a confirming and a reproach, in their claim that they were Jews or Christians; and God answered them, that He is more knowing of them than they — meaning, they were not Jews, and not Christians.

**THE TESTIMONY THAT WAS HIDDEN**

The verse then names the wrong of the claim: it is the hiding of a testimony. Mujāhid said of His saying, and who is more wrong than whoever hides a testimony he has, from God: in the saying of the Jews, of Ibrāhīm and Ismāʿīl and the ones mentioned with them, that they were Jews or Christians; and God says: do not hide, from Me, a testimony, if you have it, of them — and He knew that they were liars. Al-Ḥasan carries the testimony to its oath: he recited this verse, or do you say, Ibrāhīm and Ismāʿīl, until His saying, say, are you more knowing, or God, and who is more wrong than whoever hides a testimony he has, from God, and he said: by God, the people had, from God, a testimony, that His prophets are innocent of the Judaism and the Christianity — as the people have, from God, a testimony, that your wealth and your blood are forbidden among you; by what then did you permit them? Al-Rabīʿ said: the People of the Book hid the Islam, knowing that it is the religion of God, and they find it written with them, in the Torah and the Gospel — that they were not Jews, and not Christians; and the Judaism and the Christianity were after these, by a time. Qatādah said: those are the People of the Book — they hid the Islam, knowing that it is the religion of God, and they took the Judaism and the Christianity, and they hid Muḥammad ﷺ, knowing that he is the Messenger of God ﷺ, finding him written with them, in the Torah and the Gospel. The sūrah of Āl ʿImrān states the same argument, upon the father himself: (3:65 — **“O People of the Book! Why do you argue about Abraham”**). The verse is thus the summing of the passage of the claims: the claim is answered by the question, and the question is answered by the testimony, and the testimony is the testimony of God Himself, that the fathers were Muslims — and the one who hides it is the more wrong, because the testimony he hides is not his, and the God who gave it is not unaware of what he does.

---

## Verse 2:141

> That was a community that had already gone before. For them is what they earned and for you is what you have earned. And you will not be accountable for what they have done

**THE REPEATED VERSE**

The verse returns, word for word, the verse of the passing community. Al-Ṭabarī names its people again: God means, by His saying, that is a community: Ibrāhīm, and Ismāʿīl, and Isḥāq, and Yaʿqūb, and the Asbāṭ. Qatādah said of His saying, that is a community that has passed: He means Ibrāhīm, and Ismāʿīl, and Isḥāq, and Yaʿqūb, and the Asbāṭ; and al-Rabīʿ said the like. And He has made plain, in what passed, that the community is the group.

**THE REASON OF THE RETURN**

Al-Qurṭubī gives the repetition its reason: He repeated it because it carries the meaning of the threatening and the frightening — meaning, if those prophets, upon their imāmate and their virtue, are rewarded for their earning, you are worthier; so the confirming was obligatory, and for that reason He repeated it. Al-Ṭabarī gives the verse its full standing: say, O Muḥammad, to these who dispute with you, in God, of the Jews and the Christians, who hid the testimony they have, of the affair of Ibrāhīm and the ones We named with him, and that they were Muslims, and claimed that they were Jews or Christians, and lied: Ibrāhīm, and Ismāʿīl, and Isḥāq, and Yaʿqūb, and the Asbāṭ, are a community that has passed — meaning, it has gone its way, and come to its Lord, and passed with its deeds and its hopes; it has, with God, what it earned of good, in the days of its life, and upon it what it acquired of evil; nothing benefits it but the righteous of its deeds, and nothing harms it but their evil; so know that, O Jews and Christians — for if these, in whom you boast, and whom you claim were upon your faith, had been Jews or Christians, and their earning were the earning of the Jews and the Christians, their standing would be the standing you stand in. The repetition is thus the second striking of the same measure, upon the same claim — and the verse of the Star states the measure itself: (53:39 — **“and that each person will only have what they endeavoured towards”**). The community that passed is named twice, because the claim that ascribes it did not stop at the first naming — and the earning stands against the claim, twice.

---

## Verse 2:142

> The foolish among the people will ask, “Why did they turn away from the direction of prayer they used to face?” Say, ˹O Prophet,˺ “The east and west belong ˹only˺ to Allah. He guides whoever He wills to the Straight Path.”

**THE SAYING BEFORE IT IS SAID**

The sūrah opens the passage of the qiblah with the saying that will meet its turning. Al-Qurṭubī reads: God Most High made known that they will say, in the turning of the believers, from al-Shām, to the Kaʿbah: what turned them from it?; and they will say is in the meaning of they said — He placed the future in the place of the past, as an indication of its approaching. Mujāhid said of His saying, the foolish of the people will say, what turned them from their qiblah, which they were upon: the Jews say it, when he left Bayt al-Maqdis. Al-Suddī carries the standing of the saying: when the Prophet ﷺ was turned toward the Inviolable Masjid, the people differed in it, and they were kinds; and the hypocrites said: what is the matter of the ones who were upon a qiblah, a time, then left it, and turned toward other than it? And God sent down, concerning the hypocrites: the foolish of the people will say — the verse, all of it.

**THE SCENE OF THE TURNING**

Ibn Isḥāq carries the verse its scene, from Ibn ʿAbbās: when the qiblah was turned, from al-Shām, to the Kaʿbah — and it was turned, in Rajab, upon the head of seventeen months, from the coming of the Messenger of God ﷺ to Madīnah — there came to the Messenger of God ﷺ Rifāʿah ibn Qays, and Qardam ibn ʿAmr, and Kaʿb ibn al-Ashraf, and Nāfiʿ ibn Abī Nāfiʿ — and Abū Kurayb said: and Rāfiʿ ibn Abī Rāfiʿ — and al-Ḥajjāj ibn ʿAmr, the ally of Kaʿb ibn al-Ashraf, and al-Rabīʿ ibn al-Rabīʿ ibn Abī l-Ḥuqayq. Qatādah carries the standing of the prayer before it: the Anṣār prayed toward Bayt al-Maqdis, two years, before the coming of the Prophet ﷺ to Madīnah; and the Prophet of God ﷺ prayed, after his coming to Madīnah, emigrating, toward Bayt al-Maqdis, sixteen months; then God turned him, after that, to the Kaʿbah, the Inviolable House; and some of the people said, in that: what turned them from their qiblah, which they were upon? — the man has longed for his birthplace! And God Most High said: say, to God belong the east and the west; He guides whom He wills, to a straight path. Abū al-ʿĀliyah carries the choosing before the turning: the Prophet of God ﷺ was given the choice, to turn his face where he wished, and he chose Bayt al-Maqdis, that he might win the People of the Book; and his qiblah was sixteen months, and he was, in that, turning his face in the heaven; then God turned him toward the Inviolable House. The sūrah has stated the ownership the answer stands upon: (2:115 — **“To Allah belong the east and the west”**). The verse is thus the announcement of the saying, before the saying — and the answer of the ownership, before the objection: the foolish of the verse are named by their foolishness, because the question they ask is answered by the east and the west of the One who turns whom He wills.

---

## Verse 2:143

> And so We have made you ˹believers˺ an upright community so that you may be witnesses over humanity and that the Messenger may be a witness over you. We assigned your former direction of prayer only to distinguish those who would remain faithful to the Messenger from those who would lose faith. It was certainly a difficult test except for those ˹rightly˺ guided by Allah. And Allah would never discount your ˹previous acts of˺ faith. Surely Allah is Ever Gracious and Most Merciful to humanity

**THE MIDDLE COMMUNITY**

The verse gives the community of the turning its name. Mujāhid said of His saying, and thus We made you a middle community: just. Al-Qurṭubī reads the meaning: and as the Kaʿbah is the middle of the earth, thus We made you a middle community — meaning, We made you below the prophets, and above the nations; and the middle is the justice; and the origin of this is that the most balanced of things is their middle; and the middle of the valley is the best place in it, and the most of it in pasture and water; and since the middle is apart from the exaggerating and the falling short, it was praised — meaning, this community did not exaggerate, the exaggerating of the Christians, in their prophets, and did not fall short, the falling short of the Jews, in their prophets; and in the ḥadīth: the best of the matters is the middle of them.

**THE WITNESSING**

The witnessing of the verse is given its reports. Abū Hurayrah carries: there was brought to the Messenger of God ﷺ a funeral, and the people said: what a good one he was! And he said: it has become due! And he was asked, and he said: the angels are the witnesses of God in the heaven, and you are the witnesses of God in the earth — and whatever you testify to, becomes due; then he recited: and say, work, and God will see your work, and His Messenger, and the believers. Iyās ibn Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ carries from his father: we were with the Prophet ﷺ, and there was passed upon him a funeral, and it was praised with a good praise, and he said: it has become due! And another was passed upon him, and it was praised with less than that, and he said: it has become due! And they said: O Messenger of God, what has become due? He said: the angels are the witnesses of God in the heaven, and you are the witnesses of God in the earth. The witnessing is carried, in the report of Ḥibbān ibn Abī Jabalah, to its chain: when God gathers His servants, on the Day of Resurrection, the first called will be Isrāfīl, and his Lord will say to him: what did you do, with My covenant? Did you convey My covenant? And he will say: yes, my Lord, I conveyed it to Jibrīl; and Jibrīl will be called, and it will be said to him: did Isrāfīl convey to you My covenant? And he will say: yes, my Lord, he conveyed to me; and Isrāfīl will be released, and it will be said to Jibrīl: did you convey My covenant? And he will say: yes, I conveyed it to the messengers; and the messengers will be called, and it will be said to them: did Jibrīl convey to you My covenant? And they will say: yes, our Lord. The sūrah of the Ḥajj states the witnessing, in the same wording: (22:78 — **“so that the Messenger may be a witness over you, and that you may be witnesses over humanity”**).

**THE TRIAL AND THE FAITH**

The verse then gives the qiblah its reason: We made your qiblah, which you were upon, only that We might know who follows the Messenger, from the one who turns upon his heels. Dāwūd ibn Abī ʿĀṣim carries the question of the faith: when the Messenger of God ﷺ was turned toward the Kaʿbah, the Muslims said: our companions, who used to pray toward Bayt al-Maqdis, have perished! And God sent down: and God was not such as to let your faith go to waste. The verse carries thus the three standings of the turning: the naming of the middle, and the witnessing that follows it, and the trial that separates the following from the turning — and the faith of the ones who prayed toward the first qiblah is not lost, for the Lord of the two qiblahs is the One who turned them, and He is, to the people, the Gracious, the Merciful.

---

## Verse 2:144

> Indeed, We see you ˹O Prophet˺ turning your face towards heaven. Now We will make you turn towards a direction ˹of prayer˺ that will please you. So turn your face towards the Sacred Mosque ˹in Mecca˺—wherever you are, turn your faces towards it. Those who were given the Scripture certainly know this to be the truth from their Lord. And Allah is never unaware of what they do

**THE TURNING OF THE FACE IN THE HEAVEN**

The verse gives the turning its origin: the looking of the Messenger ﷺ, and the pleasure of his Lord. Qatādah said of His saying, We have seen the turning of your face, in the heaven: the Prophet of God ﷺ used to pray toward Bayt al-Maqdis, and he longed for, and desired, the qiblah toward the Inviolable House; and God turned him to a qiblah he longed for and desired. Al-Rabīʿ said: your looking, in the heaven; and the Prophet ﷺ used to turn his face, in the prayer, while he prayed toward Bayt al-Maqdis, and he longed for the qiblah of the Inviolable House; and God turned him to a qiblah he longed for. Al-Qurṭubī gives the verse its place in the passage: the scholars said: this verse is sent down, ahead, before His saying, the foolish of the people will say; and the meaning of the turning of your face is the turning of your face to the heaven, al-Ṭabarī said it; al-Zajjāj said: the turning of your two eyes, in the looking to the heaven; and the meaning is close.

**THE TAUNT AND THE TURNING**

Mujāhid carries the taunt that met the longing: the Jews said: Muḥammad opposes us, and follows our qiblah! And he used to supplicate God, and ask Him for the qiblah; and it was sent down: We have seen the turning of your face, in the heaven — and the saying of the Jews was cut off: he opposes us, and follows our qiblah! — in the prayer of the noon; and he made the men in the place of the women, and the women in the place of the men. Ibn Zayd carries the longer scene: God said to His Prophet Muḥammad ﷺ: so wherever you turn, there is the Face of God; and the Messenger of God ﷺ said: these are a people, the Jews, who face a house of the houses of God — to Bayt al-Maqdis — and if only we faced it! And the Prophet ﷺ faced it sixteen months, and there reached him that the Jews were saying: by God, Muḥammad and his Companions did not know where their qiblah was, until we guided them! And the Prophet ﷺ disliked that, and raised his face to the heaven; and God said: We have seen the turning of your face, in the heaven, and We will turn you to a qiblah that pleases you, so turn your face toward the Inviolable Masjid — the verse.

**THE SHATR AND ITS DIRECTION**

The direction of the command is given its word. Ibn ʿAbbās said, in the report of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭalḥah: the shatr of the Inviolable Masjid — toward it. Mujāhid said: toward it. Al-Rabīʿ said: facing it. The command of the verse is thus given to the Messenger in his person — turn your face — and to the believers in their places — and wherever you are, turn your faces toward it; and the sūrah has carried the ownership that stands over the directions, before the turning was commanded: (2:115 — **“so wherever you turn you are facing ˹towards˺ Allah”**). The verse is thus the meeting of the two turnings: the turning of the face of the Messenger in the heaven, in the longing, and the turning of the faces of the community toward the House, in the command — and between the two stands the seeing of the One who saw the turning, and answered it with the qiblah that pleases.

---

## Verse 2:145

> Even if you were to bring every proof to the People of the Book, they would not accept your direction ˹of prayer˺, nor would you accept theirs; nor would any of them accept the direction ˹of prayer˺ of another. And if you were to follow their desires after ˹all˺ the knowledge that has come to you, then you would certainly be one of the wrongdoers

**THE SIGNS THAT WOULD NOT BE FOLLOWED**

The verse closes the passage of the taunts with the news of what stands behind them. Al-Qurṭubī reads: if you bring, to those who were given the Book, every sign, they will not follow your qiblah — because they disbelieved, while the truth had become plain to them, and the signs do not benefit them — meaning, the marks; and the plural of the qiblah, in the breaking, is qibal, and in the salīm, qibilāt. Ibn Kathīr reads the standing of the verse: God describes the disbelief of the Jews, and their stubbornness, and their opposing of what they know, of the truth of the Messenger of God ﷺ — that if the Prophet ﷺ brought every proof, upon the truth of what he was sent with, they will never obey him, and never leave the following of their desires; and in another place, God said: indeed, those against whom the word of your Lord has become due will not believe — and if every sign comes to them, until they see the painful punishment. The verse is thus the severing of the hope that stood behind the dispute: the qiblah is not refused by a doubt the sign could answer, but by a decree the signs do not lift.

**THE TWO REFUSALS AND THE ONE WARNING**

The verse gives the refusal its three faces: they do not follow your qiblah, and you do not follow their qiblah, and some of them do not follow the qiblah of some. Al-Qurṭubī reads the second face: and you are not the follower of their qiblah — the wording is the wording of the report, and it carries the meaning of the command, meaning, do not incline to anything of that; then God reported that the Jews are not followers of the qiblah of the Christians, nor the Christians followers of the qiblah of the Jews — from al-Suddī and Ibn Zayd; and this is an informing of the difference of their qiblahs. Al-Ṭabarī reads the warning that closes the verse: God means, by His saying, and if you follow their desires — if you seek, O Muḥammad, the pleasure of these Jews and Christians, who said to you and to your Companions: be Jews or Christians, you will be guided — and you follow their qiblah, meaning, you return to their qiblah; and He means, by His saying, after the knowledge has come to you — after the knowledge has reached you, by My informing you that they are established upon falsehood, and in opposition to the truth, and in knowledge that the qiblah I turned you toward is the qiblah I made obligatory upon your father Ibrāhīm, and the rest of his offspring, after him, of the messengers — you would then be of the wrongdoers: meaning, if you did that, you would be of My servants who wrong themselves, and oppose My command, and leave My obedience, and one of them, and in their number. Al-Qurṭubī reads the address by its reach: the address is to the Prophet ﷺ, and what is intended is his community, of the ones for whom it is possible to follow their desire, and become, by its following, a wrongdoer; and it is not possible that the Prophet ﷺ do what he would be, by it, a wrongdoer. The sūrah of Yūnus states the decree that stands behind the refusal: (10:96 — **“Indeed, those against whom Allah’s decree ˹of torment˺ is justified will not believe”**). The verse is thus the summing of the passage of the qiblah, before the commands are repeated: the signs would not be followed, the qiblahs do not gather, and the following of the desires, after the knowledge, is the wrongdoing of the self.

---

## Verse 2:146

> Those We have given the Scripture recognize this ˹Prophet˺ as they recognize their own children. Yet a group of them hides the truth knowingly

**THE KNOWING OF THE SONS**

The verse gives the knowledge of the qiblah its standing with the scholars of the two houses. Al-Ṭabarī reads: God means, by His saying, those We gave them the Book know him: the scholars of the Jews, and the learned of the Christians; He says: these scholars, of the Jews, and the learned, of the Christians, know that the Inviolable House is their qiblah, and the qiblah of the prophets. Qatādah said of His saying, those We gave them the Book know him, as they know their sons: they know that the Inviolable House is the qiblah. Al-Rabīʿ said: He means the qiblah; and in his other report: they knew that the qiblah of the Inviolable House is their qiblah, which they were commanded with, as they knew their sons. Ibn ʿAbbās said: he means, by that, the Kaʿbah, the Inviolable House. And Mujāhid said the like. The knowing of the verse is measured by the knowing that no one denies: the knowing of the sons — and the scholars of the two houses knew the qiblah of the House, with the knowing of the fathers.

**THE HIDING THAT KNOWS**

The verse then divides the knowing from the hiding. Al-Ṭabarī reads: His saying, and they hide the truth — and that truth is the qiblah, to which God turned His Prophet Muḥammad ﷺ — He says: so turn your face toward the Inviolable Masjid — which the prophets before Muḥammad ﷺ used to turn toward; and the Jews and the Christians hid it, and some of them turned east, and some of them toward Bayt al-Maqdis, and they rejected what God commanded them with; and they hid, with that, the affair of Muḥammad ﷺ, finding him written with them, in the Torah and the Gospel; and God acquainted Muḥammad ﷺ and his community, upon their betraying God and betraying His servants, and their hiding of that, and reported that they do what they do of that, upon knowledge from them that the truth is other than it. Qatādah said of His saying, and a party of them hide the truth, while they know: they hid Muḥammad ﷺ. Mujāhid said: they hide Muḥammad ﷺ, finding him written with them, in the Torah and the Gospel. Al-Rabīʿ said: He means the qiblah. The sūrah of the Cattle states the same knowing, of the Messenger himself: (6:20 — **“Those to whom We gave the Scripture recognize him ˹to be a true prophet˺ as they recognize their own children”**). The verse carries thus the two standings of the scholars with the truth they know: the knowing of it, as the knowing of the sons, and the hiding of it, while they know — and the party that hides is named a party, because the knowing of the verse stands with some of them, and the hiding with some.

---

## Verse 2:147

> ˹This is˺ the truth from your Lord, so do not ever be one of those who doubt

**THE TRUTH, FROM THE LORD**

The verse gives the qiblah its settling. Al-Ṭabarī reads: God says: know, O Muḥammad, that the truth is what your Lord informed you of, and brought to you from Him — not what the Jews and the Christians say to you; and this is a report from God, to His Prophet, that the qiblah He turned him toward is the true qiblah, upon which was Ibrāhīm, the intimate friend of the Merciful, and the ones after him, of the prophets of God; and He says to him: work by the truth that came to you, from your Lord, O Muḥammad, and do not be of the ones who doubt. Al-Rabīʿ said: God said to His Prophet: the truth is from your Lord, so do not be of the ones who doubt — He says: do not be in doubt, for it is your qiblah, and the qiblah of the prophets, before you. Ibn Zayd said of His saying, so do not be of the ones who doubt: of the ones who doubt; he said: do not doubt of that.

**THE DOUBT THAT IS NOT HIS**

Al-Ṭabarī gives the word its root, and the prohibition its reach: the muntamir is only mufṭaʿil, from the miryah, and the miryah is the doubt; and from it is the saying of al-Aʿshā: it pours out, upon the legs of the doubters, running, when the mirage settles. And he raises the question of the prohibition: if one says to us — was the Prophet ﷺ doubting, that the truth is from his Lord, or that the qiblah God turned him toward is truth from God, until he was forbidden from the doubt in that, and it was said to him: so do not be of the ones who doubt? It is said: that is of the speech the Arabs bring out, in the going out of the command or the prohibition, to the one addressed by it, while other than him is intended by it — as God said: O Prophet, fear God, and do not obey the disbelievers and the hypocrites, then said: and follow what is revealed to you, from your Lord — the speech came out, in the going out of the command to the Prophet ﷺ, and the prohibition of him, while what is intended by it is his Companions, who believe in him. The sūrah of Yūnus gives the Messenger the same command, at the close of the same standing: (10:109 — **“And follow what is revealed to you, and be patient until Allah passes His judgment”**). The verse is thus the settling of the truth, and the closing of the doubt — and the prohibition is addressed to the one who does not doubt, that it reach the ones who can.

---

## Verse 2:148

> Everyone turns to their own direction ˹of prayer˺. So compete with one another in doing good. Wherever you are, Allah will bring you all together ˹for judgment˺. Surely Allah is Most Capable of everything

**THE DIRECTIONS OF EACH**

The verse gives the qiblahs their division. Mujāhid said of His saying, and for each is a direction: for each adherent of a religion. ʿAṭāʾ said: for each people of a religion — the Jews and the Christians; and Mujāhid said: for each adherent of a religion. Al-Rabīʿ said of His saying, and for each is a direction, he is the one who turns it: the Jew has a direction he turns to it, and the Christian has a direction he turns to it, and God guided you, O community, to the qiblah that is the qiblah. Ibn ʿAbbās said: he means, by that, the people of the religions; he says: for each is a qiblah they are pleased with; and the Face of God Most High, blessed be His name, is wherever the believers turn — and that is that God said: so wherever you turn, there is the Face of God; God is the Encompassing, the Knowing. The direction is given its face in the tongue: Mujāhid said: a direction — a qiblah; and al-Rabīʿ said: a face. Mujāhid said of His saying, he is the one who turns it: he is the one who faces it. And it is reported from Ibn ʿAbbās and other than him that they read it: he is the one who is turned to it, in the meaning that he is turned toward it; and if its doer were named, the speech would be: and for each adherent of a religion is a direction, God turns him to it, in the meaning of, He turns him toward it. And it is mentioned, of some of them, that he read it: and for each, of a direction, with the leaving of the tanwīn and the adding — and that is a mistake, and the reading by it is not permissible; because that, when read so, the report is not complete, and it is a speech that has no meaning; and that is not permissible, to be from God.

**THE RACING AND THE GATHERING**

The verse then turns from the division of the directions to the racing of the goods: for each is a direction he turns to it — so race to the goods. The directions are divided, and the racing is one: the community is not asked to answer the directions of the others, but to race to the goods — and the gathering of the verse stands behind the racing: wherever you are, God will bring you all together. The sūrah of the Table states the same division, and the same racing, upon the nations before this one: (5:48 — **“To each of you We have ordained a code of law and a way of life”**). The verse is thus the answer of the qiblah, by the standing above the qiblahs: each people has its direction, and the goods are the field of the racing, and the gathering is to the One who is over all things powerful — and the community of the middle is given its work: not the disputing of the directions, but the racing to the goods.

---

## Verse 2:149

> Wherever you are ˹O Prophet˺, turn your face towards the Sacred Mosque. This is certainly the truth from your Lord. And Allah is never unaware of what you ˹all˺ do

**THE COMMAND REPEATED**

The verse repeats the command of the qiblah, and the repetition is given its reason. Al-Ṭabarī reads: God means, by His saying, and from wherever you come out — and from whatever place you come out, to whatever place you are turned, turn, O Muḥammad, your face — He says: turn your face; and We have shown that the turning, in this place, toward the Inviolable Masjid, is only the facing of the face toward it; and We have made plain the meaning of the shatr, in what passed. And His saying, and it is the truth from your Lord, means: the turning toward its direction is the truth, in which is no doubt, from your Lord — so keep upon it, and obey God, in your turning toward it. Al-Qurṭubī gives the repetition its reason: it is said: this is a confirming of the command of the facing of the Kaʿbah, and an attending to it — because the place of the turning was very hard upon their souls, so He confirmed the command, that the people see the attending to it, and it become light upon them. The verse adds, to the command of the verse before it, the standing of the truth: the first command came with the pleasure — We will turn you to a qiblah that pleases you — and this comes with the truth — and it is the truth from your Lord; and the sūrah carries the command it repeats: (2:144 — **“wherever you are, turn your faces towards it”**). The command of the qiblah is repeated, then, as the hard thing is made light: once with the pleasure of the Messenger, and once with the truth of the Lord — and the not-unawareness, at the close of the verse, is the keeping of the turning, wherever the one who turns is.

---

## Verse 2:150

> Wherever you are ˹O Prophet˺, turn your face towards the Sacred Mosque. And wherever you ˹believers˺ are, face towards it, so that people will have no argument against you, except the wrongdoers among them. Do not fear them; fear Me, so that I may ˹continue to˺ perfect My favour upon you and so you may be ˹rightly˺ guided

**THE THIRD COMMAND AND ITS REASON**

The verse gives the command its third striking, and gives it its reason in the people. Al-Ṭabarī reads: God means, by His saying, and from wherever you come out, turn your face toward the Inviolable Masjid, and wherever you are, turn your faces toward its direction — from whatever place you came out, of the lands and the earth, and to whatever region you went, turn, O Muḥammad, and the believers, your faces, in your prayers, toward its direction, and take it a qiblah for you — that there be not, for anyone of the people, an argument against you, other than the polytheists of Quraysh. Ibn Kathīr reads the striking: this is the third command from God, with the facing of al-Masjid al-Ḥarām.

**THE ARGUMENT OF THE WRONGDOERS**

The argument the verse excepts is given its faces. Qatādah said of His saying, that there be not, for the people, an argument against you: he means, by that, the People of the Book — they said, when the Prophet of God ﷺ was turned to the Kaʿbah, the Inviolable House: the man has longed for the house of his father, and the religion of his people! Mujāhid said of His saying, except the ones who wrong, of them: the people of Muḥammad ﷺ; and he said, in another report: he says, their argument is their saying: he has returned to our qiblah! Qatādah said of the ones who wrong: the polytheists of Quraysh; he says: they will argue with you by that, and their argument, against the Prophet of God ﷺ — his turning to the Inviolable House — was their saying: he will return to our religion, as he returned to our qiblah! And God sent down, in all of that. Al-Suddī carries, in the wide chain from Abū Mālik and from Abū Ṣāliḥ, from Ibn ʿAbbās, and from Murrah al-Hamdānī, from Ibn Masʿūd, and from a people of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ: when the Prophet of God ﷺ was turned toward the Kaʿbah, after his prayer to Bayt al-Maqdis, the polytheists of the people of Makkah said: the religion of Muḥammad has bewildered him! And he turned, by his qiblah, toward you, and knew that you were more guided than him, in way; and he will soon enter your religion! And God sent down, concerning them: that there be not, for the people, an argument against you, except the ones who wrong of them — so do not fear them, and fear Me.

**THE FEAR AND THE COMPLETING**

Al-Rabīʿ carries the verse its debate, of the standing of the qiblah itself: a Jew disputed with Abū al-ʿĀliyah, and said: Mūsā used to pray toward the rock of Bayt al-Maqdis; and Abū al-ʿĀliyah said: he used to pray, at the rock, toward the Inviolable House; and he said: between me and you is the masjid of Ṣāliḥ, for he carved it from the mountain; Abū al-ʿĀliyah said: I have prayed in it, and its qiblah is toward the Inviolable House; and al-Rabīʿ said: Abū al-ʿĀliyah informed me that he passed by the masjid of Dhū l-Qarnayn, and its qiblah was toward the Kaʿbah. Al-Qurṭubī reads the fear that closes the verse: so do not fear them — He means the people — and fear Me; the khashyah, in its origin, is a settling, in the heart, that sends upon the guarding; and the khawf is a fright of the heart, by which the limbs become light; and the meaning of the verse is the belittling of every one other than God. Al-Ṭabarī reads the completing: and that I complete My blessing upon you — by My guiding of you to the qiblah of My intimate friend Ibrāhīm, whom I made, for the people, an imām — and I complete, by it, My grace upon you, and complete, by it, the laws of your faith, the ḥanīfiyyah muslimah, which I charged Nūḥ with, and Ibrāhīm, and Mūsā, and ʿĪsā, and the rest of the prophets other than them. The sūrah of the Table states the completing, upon the last of the religion: (5:3 — **“Today I have perfected your faith for you, completed My favour upon you”**). The verse is thus the closing of the passage of the qiblah: the command is struck the third time, and the argument of the wrongdoers is named and emptied, and the fear is turned from them to the One who is feared — and the completing of the blessing stands at the door of the qiblah, because the qiblah of Ibrāhīm is the last of the blessings of the faith of Ibrāhīm.

---

## Verse 2:151

> Since We have sent you a messenger from among yourselves—reciting to you Our revelations, purifying you, teaching you the Book and wisdom, and teaching you what you never knew—

**THE ANSWER OF THE SUPPLICATION**

The verse gives the qiblah its Messenger, and the Messenger his origin in the supplication of the father. Al-Ṭabarī reads the joining: God means, by His saying, as We sent, among you, a messenger from among you — and that I complete My blessing upon you, by the making plain of the laws of your faith, the ḥanīfiyyah, and that I guide you to the religion of My intimate friend Ibrāhīm; and I make, for you, his supplication with which he supplicated Me, and his asking with which he asked Me — our Lord, and make us both Muslims to You, and of our offspring a Muslim nation to You, and show us our rites, and turn to us, You are the Accepter of repentance, the Merciful — as I made, for you, his supplication with which he supplicated Me, and his asking with which he asked Me, and he said: our Lord, and raise up, among them, a messenger from among themselves, who recites to them Your signs, and teaches them the Book and the wisdom, and purifies them; so the kā-mā is attached to the saying of God, and that I complete My blessing upon you — and the saying of God, as We sent among you a messenger from among you, cannot be attached to His saying, so remember Me, I will remember you. Some said, meanwhile: the meaning is, remember Me, as We sent among you a messenger from among you, I will remember you; and this saying, though it is a going of the goings, is not the easier, the more eloquent. Mujāhid said of His saying, as We sent, among you, a messenger from among you: as I did, so remember Me.

**THE MESSENGER AND HIS FOUR DEEDS**

Al-Ṭabarī gives the verse its people and its items: the Messenger He means is the Arabs; He says to them: hold, O Arabs, to My obedience, and turn toward the qiblah I commanded you to turn toward, that the argument of the Jews be cut from you, and that there be not, for them, an argument against you, and that I complete My blessing upon you, and you be guided — as I began you with My blessing, and sent, among you, a messenger from among you; and that Messenger, whom He sent to them, from among them, is Muḥammad ﷺ. Al-Rabīʿ said of His saying, as We sent, among you, a messenger from among you: he means Muḥammad ﷺ. The four deeds of the verse are read one by one: His saying, he recites to you Our signs, means the signs of the Qurʾān; and His saying, and purifies you — and he purifies you from the filth of the sins; and he teaches you the Book — and it is the Furqān, meaning, he teaches them its rulings; and He means, by the wisdom, the sunan and the understanding in the religion. And His saying, and teaches you what you did not know, means: and he teaches you, of the news of the prophets, and the tales of the nations that passed, and the report of what is happening and being, of the matters the Arabs did not know, and they knew them, from the Messenger of God ﷺ; and He informed them that all of that is only reached by His Messenger ﷺ. The sūrah of Āl ʿImrān states the same favor, upon the same community: (3:164 — **“Indeed, Allah has done the believers a ˹great˺ favour by raising a messenger from among them”**). The verse is thus the meeting of the two supplications of Ibrāhīm, at the door of the qiblah: the Muslim nation he asked, and the Messenger he asked — and the completing of the blessing, in the verse before, is named, in this verse, by the name of the Messenger it was completed by.

---

## Verse 2:152

> remember Me; I will remember you. And thank Me, and never be ungrateful

**THE REMEMBERING AND ITS ANSWER**

The verse gives the community the exchange of the remembering. Al-Ṭabarī reads: God means, by that: remember Me, O believers, by your obeying Me, in what I command you with, and in what I forbid you from, and I will remember you, by My mercy of you, and My forgiveness of you. Saʿīd ibn Jubayr said of His saying, so remember Me, I will remember you: remember Me, by My obedience, and I will remember you, by My forgiveness. And some of them used to interpret that, of the remembering, by the praise and the commendation. Al-Suddī said of His saying, remember Me, I will remember you: there is no servant who remembers God, but God remembers him; no believer remembers Him, but He remembers him with mercy, and no disbeliever remembers Him, but He remembers him with punishment. Al-Rabīʿ carries the exchange to its three faces: God is the rememberer of the one who remembers Him, and the increaser of the one who thanks Him, and the punisher of the one who denies Him.

**THE THANKING AND ITS PROMISE**

Al-Ṭabarī reads the gratitude that follows the remembering: God means, by His saying, and thank Me, and do not deny Me: thank Me, O believers, in what I bestowed upon you, of the Islam, and the guidance to the religion I legislated for My prophets and My chosen ones; and do not deny Me — He says: do not deny My doing good to you, and I strip you of My blessing, which I bestowed upon you; but thank Me upon it, and I increase you, and complete My blessing upon you, and guide you to what I guided to, of the ones I was pleased with, of My servants; for I promised My creation that whoever thanks Me, I increase him, and whoever denies Me, I deprive him, and strip him of what I gave him. The sūrah of Ibrāhīm states the promise, upon the tongue of the Lord Himself: (14:7 — **“If you are grateful, I will certainly give you more”**). The verse is thus the two standings of the servant with his Lord, and the two answers of the Lord with His servant: the remembering that is answered by the remembering, and the thanking that is answered by the increase — and the denial is the standing that meets the stripping, for the blessing that is not thanked is the blessing that is not kept.

---

## Verse 2:153

> O believers! Seek comfort in patience and prayer. Allah is truly with those who are patient

**THE SEEKING OF HELP**

The verse gives the community its two provisions, for the standing it is entering. Al-Ṭabarī reads: this verse is an urging, from God, upon His obedience, and the bearing of its disliked, upon the bodies and the wealths; and He said: O you who believe, seek help, by patience and prayer, upon the standing with My obedience, and the paying of My obligations, in the abrogating of My rulings, and the turning, from what I abrogate of them, to what I bring, for you, of My obligations, and what I move you to, of My rulings, and the submitting to My command, in what I command you with, at the time of My making its ruling obligatory upon you, and the turning from it, after My turning you from it — though there reach you, in that, a disliked, of the saying of your enemies of the disbelievers, by their casting upon you the falsehood, or a hardship, upon your bodies, in your standing with it, or a decrease, in your wealths — and upon the striving against your enemies. Abū al-ʿĀliyah said, in the report of al-Rabīʿ: seek help, by patience and prayer, upon the pleasure of God, and know that the two are of the obedience of God. Al-Rabīʿ said: know that the two are a help, upon the obedience of God. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Zayd ibn Aslam gives the patience its two halves: the patience has two parts — the patience, for God, in what He is pleased with, of the acts of worship and the obedience, though it be hard upon the heart and the body, and the patience, in the avoiding of what He dislikes, though it be desired; and the ones who acquire these qualities are of the people of the patience.

**THE WITH-NESS OF THE LORD**

The verse closes with the with-ness that stands behind the seeking. Al-Ṭabarī reads: His saying, God is with the patient, its interpretation is: God is his helper, and his supporter, and pleased with his deed — as the saying of the speaker: do, O so-and-so, such and such, and I am with you — meaning, I am your helper, upon that deed of yours, and your assister upon it. The sūrah of Āl ʿImrān gives the community the four commands of the same standing: (3:200 — **“O believers! Patiently endure, persevere, stand on guard”**). The verse is thus the provision of the passage of the qiblah, and the passage of the trial that follows it: the patience that bears the turning of the rulings, and the prayer that holds the face of the servant toward the One who turns them — and the with-ness of God is the standing of the Lord, with the ones who stand with His command.

---

## Verse 2:154

> Never say that those martyred in the cause of Allah are dead—in fact, they are alive! But you do not perceive it

**THE PROHIBITION OF THE WORD**

The verse forbids the word of the death, upon the ones who were killed in the way of God. Mujāhid said of His saying, rather, alive: with their Lord, they are provided for, from the fruits of the Garden, and they find its scent, and they are not in it. Al-Rabīʿ said of His saying, and do not say, of the ones who are killed in the way of God, they are dead; rather, alive: in the forms of green birds, they fly, in the Garden, wherever they wish of it, and eat, wherever they wish.
