Al-Qurṭubī reads the verse by its sister: this is the like of His saying, and do not think, of the ones who were killed in the way of God, that they are dead; rather, alive, with their Lord, provided for; and there, the speech will come, of the martyrs and their rulings.

**THE PARTICULARITY OF THE MARTYR**

Al-Ṭabarī raises the question of the particularity, and answers it: if one says to us — what is in His saying, and do not say, of the ones who are killed in the way of God, they are dead; rather, alive, of the particularity of the report of the one killed in the way of God, that is not generalized to other than him? And you have known the reports, from the Messenger of God ﷺ, that he described the state of the believers and the disbelievers, after their death — and reported, of the believers, that there are opened for them, from their graves, doors to the Garden, and they smell its coolness from them, and hasten God with the standing of the Hour, that they come to their dwellings of it, and are gathered, between them, and their families and their children, in it; and of the disbelievers, that there are opened for them, from their graves, doors to the Fire, and they look to it, and there reaches them of its stench — it is said: what God particularized the martyrs with, in that, and benefited the believers, by His report of them, is His informing them that they are provided for, of the foods of the Garden and its meals, in their barzakh, before their raising, and delighted with what the ones who enter it, after the raising, of the rest of mankind, are delighted with, of the delicious of its meals, which God has not fed, in his barzakh, before his raising, anyone other than them; and that is the virtue He preferred them by, and particularized them with, without other than them. Ibn ʿAbbās carries the report of the standing: the Messenger of God ﷺ said: the martyrs are upon Bāriq, a river at the door of the Garden, in a green tent — and ʿAbdah said: in a green meadow — and there comes out to them their provision, from the Garden, morning and evening. The sūrah of Āl ʿImrān states the same life, in the same wording: (3:169 — **“Never think of those martyred in the cause of Allah as dead”**). The verse is thus the lifting of the word of the death, from the ones who were killed in the way of God — and the life it establishes is not the life of the world that perceives, but the life of the barzakh that provides, and the community is forbidden from the saying of the death, because the saying of the death is the saying of the perceiving, and the life of the verse is beyond the perceiving.

---

## Verse 2:155

> We will certainly test you with a touch of fear and famine and loss of property, life, and crops. Give good news to those who patiently endure—

**THE ANNOUNCEMENT OF THE TRIAL**

The verse announces the trial, before it comes. Al-Ṭabarī reads: this is an informing, from God, the followers of His Messenger ﷺ, that He is trying them and testing them, with hardnesses of the matters, that He may know who follows the Messenger, of the one who turns upon his heels — as He tried them, and tested them, by the turning of the qiblah, from Bayt al-Maqdis, to the Kaʿbah, and as He tested His chosen ones, before them; and He promised them that, in another verse, and said to them: or did you think that you will enter the Garden, while there has not come to you the like of the ones who passed, before you? Misery and hardship touched them, and they were shaken, until the Messenger says, and the ones who believed with him: when is the help of God? — truly, the help of God is near. Ibn ʿAbbās said, in the report of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭalḥah: God informed the believers that the world is the house of trial, and that He is testing them in it, and commanded them with the patience, and gave them the good news, and said: and give good news to the patient; then He informed them that He did this, with His prophets and His chosen ones, that their souls be at ease, and He said: misery and hardship touched them, and they were shaken. ʿAṭāʾ said of His saying: they are the Companions of Muḥammad ﷺ.

**THE ITEMS OF THE TRIAL**

The trial is given its items, one by one. Al-Ṭabarī reads: His saying, with something of the fear, means, of the fear of the enemy; and with the hunger — and it is the drought; He says: We will test you, with something of a fear that reaches you, of your enemy, and with a year that strikes you, in which there reaches you hunger and hardship, and the demands are hard upon you, and your wealths decrease for that, and wars that will be, between you and your enemies of the disbelievers, and your number decreases for them, and the death of your children and your offspring, and barrennesses that happen, and your fruits decrease for them; all of that is an address, from Him, to the followers of His Messenger ﷺ and his Companions. Al-Rabīʿ said of His saying, and We will test you, with something of the fear and the hunger, and a decrease, of the wealths, and the souls, and the fruits: that has been, and there will be, what is more severe than that. And God said, with something of the fear, and did not say, with things, for the difference of the kinds. Qurṭubī gives the trial its tongue: al-balāʾ is good, and it is bad; and its root is the test.

**THE GOOD NEWS OF THE PATIENT**

The verse closes with the command of the good news: God said, then, to His Prophet ﷺ: O Muḥammad, give good news to the patient, upon My testing them, with what I test them with, and the ones who keep their souls, from the advancing upon My prohibition, of what I forbade them from, and the ones who take their souls, with the paying of what I obligate them with, of My obligations, with My testing them, with what I test them with, and the ones who say, when a calamity strikes them: we are God's, and to Him we return; and God commanded him to particularize, with the good news, upon what He tests them with, of the hardnesses, the people of the patience, whose attribute God described. The sūrah of Muḥammad states the trial, by its purpose: (47:31 — **“until We prove those of you who ˹truly˺ struggle ˹in Allah’s cause˺ and remain steadfast”**). The verse is thus the map of the road the community walks, after the qiblah: the trial that is announced, the items that are named, and the good news that is given, before the striking — for the one who knows the trial, before it comes, meets it with the patience of the one who was told.

---

## Verse 2:156

> who, when faced with a disaster, say, “Surely to Allah we belong and to Him we will ˹all˺ return.”

**THE WORD OF THE STRUCK**

The verse gives the patience of the verse before its word. Al-Ṭabarī reads: God means, by that: and give good news, O Muḥammad, to the patient, who know that all that is in them, of blessing, is from Me, and they confess My servitude, and single Me in the Lordship, and affirm the return and the coming back to Me, and submit to My decree, and hope My reward, and fear My punishment, and say, when I test them, with some of My tests, and try them, with what I promised them, that I would try them with, of the fear, and the hunger, and the decrease of the wealths, and the souls, and the fruits, and other than that, of the calamities I test them with: we are the property of our Lord and our Worshipped One, living, and we are His servants, and to Him, after our death, we are returning — a submitting to My decree, and a contentment with My rulings. Al-Qurṭubī gives the word its root: the muṣībah is all that harms the believer, and strikes him; one says, it struck him, a striking, and a muṣābah, and a muṣāb; and the muṣībah is the one of the maṣāʾib.

**THE REFUGE OF THE TRIED**

Al-Qurṭubī gives the word its standing with the community: God made these words a refuge, for the ones of the calamities, and a protection, for the ones who are tested — for what they gather, of the blessed meanings; for His saying, we are God's, is a singling, and a confessing of the servitude; and the rest of the verse is the return. The word of the verse is thus the two confessions of the struck: the confession of the belonging, in the first half — we are God's — and the confession of the returning, in the second — and to Him we return; and between the two stands the calamity, held by the two confessions. The sūrah of the Groups gives the patience its reward: (39:10 — **“Only those who endure patiently will be given their reward without limit”**). The verse is the smallest of the forms of the patience, and the greatest of their returns: a word, said when the calamity strikes, in which is the singling of the Lord, and the submitting to the decree, and the hope of the reward — and the word is not asked of the one who was not struck, for it is the word of the striking itself.

---

## Verse 2:157

> They are the ones who will receive Allah’s blessings and mercy. And it is they who are ˹rightly˺ guided

**THE THREE GIFTS**

The verse gives the word of the struck its answer, from the Lord. Al-Ṭabarī reads: God means, by His saying, those ones — these patient ones, whom He described and characterized — upon them, meaning, for them, blessings — meaning, forgiveness; and the blessing of God, upon His servants, is His forgiving of His servants — as is reported from the Prophet ﷺ, that he said: O God, bless the family of Abī Awfā — meaning, forgive them; and His saying, and mercy, means: and for them, with the forgiveness, by which He pardons their sins and covers them, is a mercy from God, and a tenderness; then He reported, with what He mentioned, of His giving them, upon their patience, upon His tests, submitting to His decree, of the forgiveness and the mercy, that they are the rightly guided, the ones who hit the way of the truth, and the ones who say what is pleased with them, and do what they deserve, by it, from God, the abundant of the reward. Al-Rabīʿ said of His saying, upon them are blessings, from their Lord, and mercy: he says: the blessings and the mercy are upon the ones who were patient, and returned.

**THE REPORT OF THE RETURNING**

Ibn ʿAbbās carries, in the report of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭalḥah, the verse its counting: God reported that the believer, when he surrenders the affair to God, and returns, and does the returning, at the calamity, there is written for him three qualities of the good: the blessing, from God, and the mercy, and the verifying of the way of the guidance; and the Messenger of God ﷺ said: whoever does the returning, at the calamity, God mends his calamity, and makes his outcome good, and makes for him a righteous successor, that he is pleased with. Saʿīd ibn Jubayr carries the verse its standing, with the community: no one was given what this community was given: those who, when a calamity strikes them, say, we are God's, and to Him we return — upon them are blessings, from their Lord, and mercy; and if it had been given to anyone, it would have been given to Yaʿqūb; have you not heard, of His saying: O my sorrow, upon Yūsuf? The sūrah of the Confederates gives the blessing its form, upon the Prophet himself: (33:56 — **“Indeed, Allah showers His blessings upon the Prophet, and His angels pray for him”**). The verse is thus the answer of the word of the returning: the three gifts, named by their names — the blessing, and the mercy, and the guidance — and the report of the Prophet ﷺ adds to the three the mending of the calamity, and the good of the outcome, and the successor that is pleased with — for the word that confesses the belonging, is answered by the giving of the Owner.

---

## Verse 2:158

> Indeed, ˹the hills of˺ Ṣafa and Marwah are among the symbols of Allah. So whoever performs the pilgrimage or minor pilgrimage, let them walk between ˹the two hills˺. And whoever does good willingly, Allah is truly Appreciative, All-Knowing

**THE QUESTION OF THE ANṢĀR**

The verse gives the two hills their standing, after the standing of the qiblah. ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr carries the scene, from ʿĀʾishah: I asked ʿĀʾishah, and I said to her: what do you say, of the saying of God, Ṣafā and Marwah are of the symbols of God, so whoever performs the ḥajj of the House, or the ʿumrah, there is no sin upon him, that he circumambulate by the two? — and I said to ʿĀʾishah: by God, there is no sin upon anyone, that he not circumambulate by Ṣafā and al-Marwah! And she said: a bad thing you have said, O son of my sister! This verse, if it were as you interpreted it, would be: there is no sin upon him, that he not circumambulate by the two; but it was only sent down, concerning the Anṣār — they used, before they embraced Islam, to raise their voices, for Manāt the tyrant; and in the report of Maʿmar, from al-Zuhrī: there were men, of the Anṣār, of the ones who raised their voices for Manāt, in the jāhiliyyah — and Manāt is an idol, between Makkah and Madīnah; and they said: O Prophet of God, we used not to circumambulate between Ṣafā and al-Marwah, in the honoring of Manāt; is there upon us any sin, that we circumambulate by the two? And God sent down: Ṣafā and Marwah are of the symbols of God, so whoever performs the ḥajj of the House, or the ʿumrah, there is no sin upon him, that he circumambulate by the two. ʿUrwah said: I said to ʿĀʾishah: I do not mind, not circumambulating between Ṣafā and al-Marwah! God said: there is no sin upon him. And she said: O son of my brother, do you not see that He said: there is no sin upon him, that he circumambulate by the two — and He did not say: there is no sin upon him, that he not circumambulate by the two?

Anas ibn Mālik carries the standing, in the report of al-Bukhārī, through ʿĀṣim ibn Sulaymān: I asked Anas ibn Mālik of Ṣafā and al-Marwah, and he said: we used to see that the two were of the affair of the jāhiliyyah; and when the Islam came, we held back from the two; and God sent down: Ṣafā and Marwah are of the symbols of God, to the end of the verse.

**THE SYMBOLS AND THEIR WALKING**

Al-Qurṭubī reads the verse by its items: there is no sin upon him — meaning, no sin; and its root is from the junūḥ, and it is the inclining; and the verification of the saying, in it, is that the saying of the speaker — and he gives the answer of ʿĀʾishah. And al-Tirmidhī reported, from Jābir, that the Prophet ﷺ, when he came to Makkah, circumambulated by the House, seven, and read: and take, of the maqām of Ibrāhīm, a place of prayer, and prayed, behind the maqām; then he came to the Corner, and touched it; then he said — and went out to Ṣafā. The verse is thus read by the two standings: the standing of the permission, that answered the scruple of the Anṣār, and the standing of the practice, that made the walking of the Prophet ﷺ the form of the walking of the community. The sūrah of the Ḥajj states the law of the symbols, over the two hills: (22:32 — **“And whoever honours the symbols of Allah, it is certainly out of the piety of the heart”**). The verse is thus the joining of the House to its two hills: the qiblah of the prayer was named, and the maqām of the prayer was named, and now the walking between Ṣafā and al-Marwah is named — and the symbols of God are the family of the House, in the worship of the community.

---

## Verse 2:159

> Those who hide the clear proofs and guidance that We have revealed—after We made it clear for humanity in the Book—will be condemned by Allah and ˹all˺ those who condemn

**THE DELEGATION THAT WAS ANSWERED WITH THE HIDING**

Ibn Isḥāq carries the verse its occasion, from Ibn ʿAbbās: Muʿādh ibn Jabal, the brother of Banū Salamah, and Saʿd ibn Muʿādh, the brother of Banū ʿAbd al-Ashhal, and Khārijah ibn Zayd, the brother of Banū l-Ḥārith ibn al-Khazraj, asked a group of the scholars of the Jews — Abū Kurayb said: about what is in the Torah; and Ibn Ḥumayd said: about some of what is in the Torah — and they hid it from them, and refused to inform them of it; and God sent down, concerning them: those who hide what We sent down, of the clear proofs and the guidance, from after We made it plain, for the people, in the Book. Al-Ṭabarī reads the hiding by its object: God means, by the guidance, what He made plain, for them, of His affair, in the Books He sent down to their prophets; and God says: those who hide, from the people, what We sent down, in their Books, of the making plain, of the affair of Muḥammad ﷺ, and his prophethood, and the soundness of the faith He sent him with, and its rightness, and do not inform them of it, and do not declare, of My making plain of that, for the people, and My making it plain, for them, in the Book I sent down to their prophets. Mujāhid said: they are the People of the Book. Al-Rabīʿ said: they hid Muḥammad ﷺ, finding him written with them, and they hid him, in envy and transgression. Qatādah said: those are the People of the Book — they hid the Islam, and it is the religion of God, and they hid Muḥammad ﷺ, finding him written with them, in the Torah and the Gospel.

**THE GENERALITY OF THE HIDING**

Al-Ṭabarī gives the verse its reach: this verse, though it was sent down, concerning particular ones of the people, is intended, by it, every hider of a knowledge God made obligatory the making plain of, for the people. Abū Hurayrah carries the verse its standing, with the narrating: were it not for two verses God sent down, in His Book, I would not have narrated a thing: those who hide what We sent down, of the clear proofs, to the end of the verse; and the other verse: and when God took the covenant of the ones who were given the Book. Mujāhid said of His saying, and the ones who curse curse them: the ones who curse are the beasts. The sūrah of Āl ʿImrān states the covenant the hiding breaks: (3:187 — **“to make it known to people and not hide it”**). The verse is thus the curse of the hiding, upon the two faces of the hiding: the hiding of the affair of the Messenger, from the Books, and the hiding of the knowledge God made obligatory the making plain of — and the ones who curse, of the verse, are named generally, because the hiding is the wronging of all the ones for whom the knowledge was made plain.

---

## Verse 2:160

> As for those who repent, mend their ways, and let the truth be known, they are the ones to whom I will turn ˹in forgiveness˺, for I am the Accepter of Repentance, Most Merciful

**THE EXCEPTION OF THE RETURNING**

The verse gives the curse of the hiding its door of the returning. Al-Ṭabarī reads: God means, by that: God, and the ones who curse, curse the ones who hide, from the people, what they knew, of the affair of the prophethood of Muḥammad ﷺ, and his attribute, and his description, in the Book God sent down, and made plain, for the people — except the one who returns, of his hiding of that, of them, and reviews the repentance, by the belief in Muḥammad ﷺ, and the acknowledging of him, and of his prophethood, and the affirming of him, in what he brought from God, and the making plain of what God sent down, in His Books, that He sent down to His prophets, of the command of his following; and mends the state of his self, by the coming near to God, of the righteous of the deeds, with what pleases Him with him; and makes plain what he knew, of the revelation of God, that He sent down to His prophets, and covenanted with them, in His Books, and does not hide it, and shows it. Qatādah said of His saying, except the ones who repent, and mend, and make plain: he says, they mend, in what is between them and God, and make plain what came to them, from God, and do not hide it, and do not deny it: those I repent to, and I am the Accepter of repentance, the Merciful. Ibn Zayd said: they make plain what is in the Book of God, for the believers, and what they asked them of, of the affair of the Prophet ﷺ; and this is all of it, in the Jews.

**THE QUESTION OF THE TWO REPENTINGS**

Al-Ṭabarī raises the question of the wording, and answers it: if one says — how is the one who repents repented to? And what is the face of His saying, except the ones who repent — those I repent to them? Is there a repentant, except that he is repented to, or a repented to, except that he is repenting? It is said: that is of what one of the two is not, except the other is with it — and it is the same, whether it be said, except the ones who are repented to, and they repent, or except the ones who repent, I repent to them. And some claimed that the meaning of His saying, and they make plain, is only: and they make plain the repentance, by the sincerity of the deed; and the evidence of the apparent of the Book, and the sending down, is contrary to it — for the people were only reproached, before this verse, for their hiding of what God sent down, and made plain, in His Book, of the affair of Muḥammad ﷺ and his religion; then He excepted, of them, the ones who make plain the affair of Muḥammad ﷺ and his religion, and repent, of what they were upon, of the denying and the hiding, and He brought them out of the number of the ones whom God curses, and the ones who curse curse. And the ones God excepted, of the ones who hide what God sent down, of the clear proofs and the guidance, are ʿAbd Allāh ibn Salām, and the ones with him, of the People of the Book, who embraced Islam, and their Islam was good, and they followed the Messenger of God ﷺ. Al-Qurṭubī gives the repentance its measure: the repentance is not enough, with our scholars, by the saying of the speaker, I have repented — until there appears, from him, in the second, the contrary of the first. The sūrah of the Women states the same returning, upon the same steps: (4:146 — **“except those who repent, mend their ways, hold fast to Allah”**). The verse is thus the door the curse leaves open: the hiding is answered by the curse, and the curse is lifted by the three — the repenting, and the mending, and the making plain; and the making plain stands among the three, because the sin was the hiding, and the repentance of the hiding is only the showing of what was hidden.

---

## Verse 2:161

> Surely those who disbelieve and die as disbelievers are condemned by Allah, the angels, and all of humanity

**THE ENDING THAT SEALS THE HIDING**

The verse closes the affair of the hiders by joining them to all who die upon denial. Al-Ṭabarī reads: God means, by His saying, those who disbelieve: those who deny the prophethood of Muḥammad ﷺ and call him a liar, of the Jews and the Christians and the rest of the people of the creeds, and the associators of the worshippers of idols; and they die while they are disbelievers, meaning: and they die upon their denial of that, and their calling Muḥammad ﷺ a liar; upon them is the curse of God, meaning: God removes them, and drives them away, from His mercy, and the angels, meaning: the angels curse them, and all of mankind; and the cursing of the angels and of mankind is their saying: upon them be the curse of God. Al-Qurṭubī gives the curse its root: the laʿnah is the banishment and the driving away, from God.

**WHO CURSES THE ONE ALL CURSE**

Al-Ṭabarī raises the question of the verse, and gives it its answers: if one says: how is there, upon the one who dies a disbeliever in Muḥammad ﷺ, the cursing of all of mankind, from the varieties of the nations, when most of them are of the ones who do not believe in him and do not verify him? It is said: the meaning of that is other than what you have gone to. And the people of interpretation differed in the interpretation of that: some said God meant, by His saying, and all of mankind, the people of belief in Him and in His Messenger, in particular, to the exclusion of the rest of the humans. Qatādah said of His saying, and all of mankind: He means, by all of mankind, the believers. Al-Rabīʿ said the like of it. Abū al-ʿĀliyah carries the verse its scene: the disbeliever is made to stand, on the Day of Resurrection, and God curses him, then the angels curse him, then all of mankind curse him. Al-Suddī gives it its reach: there is no two who curse one another, of the two believers, or of the two disbelievers, but one of them says: God curse the wrongdoer — except that that curse falls upon the disbeliever, because he is the wrongdoer; so every one of the creation curses him.

Al-Ṭabarī gives the verse its verdict: the first of these sayings, in correctness, is the saying of Abū al-ʿĀliyah — for every one of the children of Ādam is not prevented from saying that, whoever he is, and of whichever people of creed he is, so there enters, by that, into his cursing, every disbeliever, whoever he is; and because God informed, of the ones who witness them, on the Day of Resurrection, that they curse them: and who is more wrong than the one who fabricates a lie upon God, those are the ones presented before their Lord, and the witnesses say: these are the ones who lied upon their Lord; indeed the curse of God is upon the wrongdoers. The sūrah of Āl ʿImrān states the same curse, upon the same ones: (3:87 — **“condemned by Allah, the angels, and all of humanity”**). The verse is thus the joining of the hiders of the verse before to the whole of the dying disbelievers: the hiding was the work of the hand, and the dying upon the denial is the seal of the heart, and the curse of the verse is the answer of both.

---

## Verse 2:162

> They will be in Hell forever. Their punishment will not be lightened, nor will they be delayed ˹from it˺

**THE ETERNITY OF THE CURSE**

The verse gives the curse its duration. Al-Ṭabarī reads it by its grammar, first: if one says: what installed, abiding therein? It is said: it is installed as the ḥāl, of the hāʾ and the mīm, the two that are in His saying, upon them; and the meaning of His saying, upon them is the curse of God: those ones, God curses them, and the angels, and all of mankind, while they are abiding therein. And as for the hāʾ and the alif, the two that are in His saying, therein, then they return upon the curse, and the speech is intended by that: what the disbeliever has come to, by the curse of God, and His angels, and mankind — and what he has come to, by it, is the fire of Jahannam; and the speech was run upon the curse, and what is meant by it is what the disbeliever has come to, by it. Abū al-ʿĀliyah said: abiding therein — meaning, in Jahannam — in the curse; and al-Rabīʿ said of His saying, abiding therein: abiding in Jahannam, in the curse.

**THE THREE DENIALS**

Al-Ṭabarī reads the three clauses of the verse: and as for His saying, the punishment will not be lightened for them, then it is a report, from God, of the permanence of the punishment, forever, without timing and without lightening; and as for His saying, nor are they reprieved, then He means: they are not reprieved, with an excuse by which they excuse themselves. The verse thus holds the curse in three denials: the denial of the cutting off — abiding therein; the denial of the lightening — the punishment will not be lightened for them; and the denial of the reprieve — nor are they reprieved. The sūrah of the Ornaments states the same eternity, and adds to it the despair: (43:75 — **“It will never be lightened for them, and there they will be overwhelmed with despair”**). The verse is the second half of the affair of the dying disbeliever: the curse of the verse before named the ones who curse, and this verse names the duration of what the curse brings — and the duration is eternity, and the eternity is without the three reliefs that the punished hope for: the cutting off, and the lightening, and the reprieve.

---

## Verse 2:163

> Your God is ˹only˺ One God. There is no god ˹worthy of worship˺ except Him—the Most Compassionate, Most Merciful

**THE FIRST THING THAT MAY NOT BE HIDDEN**

Al-Qurṭubī gives the verse its place in the sūrah: when God warned, of the hiding of the truth, He made plain that the first of what it is obligatory to make plain, and not permitted to hide, is the affair of the singling; and He joined to that the mentioning of the proof, and the knowledge of the way of reflection — and it is the reflecting upon the marvels of the making — to make known that it must have a Maker that nothing resembles. Ibn ʿAbbās gives the verse its occasion: the disbelievers of Quraysh said: O Muḥammad, give us the lineage of your Lord! And God sent down the sūrah of the Sincerity, and this verse; and the associators had three hundred and sixty idols, and God made plain that He is one. Al-Ṭabarī reads the verse by its meaning: the one who deserves, over you, O people, the obedience to Him, and the worship from you, is one worshipped, and one Lord; so do not worship other than Him, and do not associate with Him other than Him; for the one you associate with Him, in your worship of Him, is a created thing, of the creation of your worshipped, the like of you; and your worshipped is one worshipped, having no like and no peer.

**THE FOUR MEANINGS OF THE ONE**

Al-Ṭabarī carries the oneness its verification: the people differed, of the meaning of His oneness; some said: the meaning of the oneness of God is the meaning of the negating, from Him, of the likes and the peers — as one says: so-and-so is the one of the people, meaning: he has not his like, among the people, and he has not his peer, among his folk; and they claimed that what guided them to the soundness of their interpretation was that the saying of the sayer, one, is understood in four meanings: the one of them is that it be one, of a genus, like the one human, of the humans; and the other, that it be not divided, like the part that does not divide; and the third, that it be intended by it the likeness and the agreement, like the saying of the sayer: these two things are one, meaning: they are resembling, until they became, by their resemblance, like one thing; and the fourth: that it be intended, by it, the negating, from Him, of the peer and the likeness; and when the three meanings, of the meanings of the one, were negated from Him, the fourth meaning, that we described, was sound. And others said: the meaning of His oneness is the meaning of His singling, from the things, and the singling of the things, from Him — for He is not entering in a thing, and no thing enters in Him. And His saying, there is no god but He, is a report, from Him, that there is no Lord of the worlds, other than Him, and none deserves, over the servants, the worship, other than Him; and all that is other than Him is His creation, and the obligatory, upon all of them, is the obeying of Him, and the submitting to His command, and the abandoning of the worship of the idols and the images. Al-Qurṭubī reads His saying, there is no god but He, by its composition: it is a negation, and an affirmation — the first of it is disbelief, and the last of it is faith, and its meaning is: there is none worshipped, except God; and God promised, of the reward, the abundant, for the one who says it, upon the tongue of His Prophet ﷺ — it was brought out by the Muwaṭṭaʾ, and al-Bukhārī, and Muslim, and others besides them; and the Prophet ﷺ said: whoever is, of his last speech, there is no god but God, enters Paradise — Muslim brings it out; and the intended is the heart, not the tongue. Kathīr carries the verse its report: Shahr ibn Ḥawshab reported that Asmāʾ bint Yazīd ibn al-Sakan narrated that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: the greatest name of God is in these two verses: your God is one God, there is no god but He, the Compassionate, the Merciful — and: Alif Lām Mīm, God, there is no god but He, the Living, the Sustainer. The sūrah of the Prophets states the same oneness, in the tongue of the Messenger: (21:108 — **“Your God is only One God”**). The verse is thus the first thing the hiding may not hide: the oneness of the Worshipped — and it is answered, in the verse that follows, by the showing of its proofs.

---

## Verse 2:164

> Indeed, in the creation of the heavens and the earth; the alternation of the day and the night; the ships that sail the sea for the benefit of humanity; the rain sent down by Allah from the skies, reviving the earth after its death; the scattering of all kinds of creatures throughout; the shifting of the winds; and the clouds drifting between the heavens and the earth—˹in all of this˺ are surely signs for people of understanding

**THE SIGN THAT WAS ASKED FOR**

The verse gives the oneness of the verse before its proof. Al-Ṭabarī carries its occasion, in the report of Saʿīd ibn Masrūq, from Abū l-Ḍuḥā: when your God is one God, there is no god but He, the Compassionate, the Merciful came down, the associators said: if it is thus, then let him bring us a sign! And God sent down: indeed, in the creation of the heavens and the earth, and the alternation of the night and the day — the verse. ʿAṭāʾ carries the like of it: the associators said to the Prophet ﷺ: show us a sign! And this verse came down. Al-Qurṭubī gives the scene its other face: when your God is one God came down, the disbelievers of Quraysh said: how does one God suffice the people? And He sent down: indeed, in the creation of the heavens and the earth; and it was reported, from Ibn Jurayj, from ʿAṭāʾ: the associators asked the Jews, and they said: tell us of what came to you from Mūsā, of the signs — and the verse came down. Al-Ṭabarī reads the verse by its address: O associators, if you were ignorant, or doubted, of the truth of what I informed you of, that your God is one God, to the exclusion of what you claim, of the divinity of the rivals and the idols, then reflect upon My proofs, and think upon them — for of My proofs is the creation of the heavens and the earth, and the alternation of the night and the day, and the ships that run in the sea, with what benefits the people, and what I sent down, of the water, from the heaven, and I gave life, by it, to the earth, after its death, and what I spread, in it, of the moving creatures, and the turning of the winds, and the clouds that are made to serve, between the heaven and the earth.

**THE SIGNS AND THEIR READING**

Al-Qurṭubī gives the verse its fourteen questions, beginning with the two great signs: the sign of the heavens is their raising, without pillars, beneath them, and without suspendings, above them — and if a prophet came, and were challenged, with the standing of a mountain, in the air, without a suspending, it would be a miracle; then what is in them, of the sun and the moon and the stars, journeying, and the planets, shining, rising and setting, luminous and dimmed, is a second sign; and the sign of the earth is its seas, and its rivers, and its minerals, and its trees, and its plain, and its ruggedness; and He pluralized the heavens, because they are differing genera — every heaven is of a genus, other than the genus of the other — and He made the earth one, because it is all dust. And the alternation of the night and the day is their coming and going, of the one of them, and the turning away of the other, from where it is not known; and it is said: their differing, in the attributes, of the light and the darkness, and the length and the shortness. Ibn al-Aʿrābī said: the نسيم is the first blowing of the wind; and the root of the wind is روح, and for that it is said, in the plural of the few, أرواح, and it is not said أرياح, because it is of the ones of the wāw. The sūrah of Āl ʿImrān repeats the same opening, and names its people: (3:190 — **“there are signs for people of reason”**). The verse is thus the answer of the asking for the sign: the sign was not brought from outside the creation, because the creation itself is the sign — and the asking for the sign is answered by the pointing to the seven, that the sūrah numbers, one after the other, until the pointing reaches the people of the understanding, who are its people.

---

## Verse 2:165

> Still there are some who take others as Allah’s equal—they love them as they should love Allah—but the ˹true˺ believers love Allah even more. If only the wrongdoers could see the ˹horrible˺ punishment ˹awaiting them˺, they would certainly realize that all power belongs to Allah and that Allah is indeed severe in punishment

**THE RIVALS AND THEIR LOVE**

The verse returns, from the proof of the oneness, to the ones who set up the rivals against it. Mujāhid said, in the report of Ibn Abī Najīḥ: they love them, like the love of God — in rivalry, and in matching, of the truth, by the rivals; and those who believe are stronger in love for God — than the disbelievers, for their idols; and al-Rabīʿ said: they are the gods that are worshipped, other than God, meaning: they love their idols, like the love of God, and those who believe are stronger in love for God — meaning: than the disbelievers, for their idols. Al-Qurṭubī carries the love its faces: al-Mubarrid said: they love their idols, upon the falsehood, like the love of the believers, for God, upon the truth; and al-Zajjāj said its meaning: they, with the impotence of the idols, love them, like the love of the believers, for God, with His power; and Ibn ʿAbbās and al-Suddī said: what is meant, by the rivals, are the heads, the ones followed — they obey them, in the disobeying of God; and the pronoun, in they love them, came, upon this, upon the origin; and upon the first, the pronoun of the idols came, the pronoun of the ones who reason, other than upon the origin. Ibn Kaysān and al-Zajjāj said: the meaning of they love them, like the love of God, is: they equate, between the idols and God, in the love; Abū Isḥāq said: this saying is the correct one, and the evidence, of its soundness, is: and those who believe are stronger in love for God.

**THE SEEING THAT WOULD KNOW**

Al-Ṭabarī carries the second half of the verse, its reading and its meaning: and some read: and if you see, O Muḥammad, those who did wrong, when they see the punishment, that the power is God's, all together, and that God is severe in the punishment — and it is the reading of the ones who read, and if you see, with the tāʾ; and others read: and if those who did wrong see, when they see the punishment, that the power is God's, all together — meaning: if they were to see, with their eyes, the punishment of God, they would know the state they come to; then He informed, a new report, of His power and His authority: that the power is God's, all together, in this world and the next, to the exclusion of the rivals and the idols, and that God is severe in punishment, for the one who associated with Him, and claimed, with Him, partners, and made, for Him, a rival. Al-Qurṭubī gives the verse its place: when God informed, in the verse before, of what guided, upon His oneness, and His power, and the greatness of His authority, He informed that, with these signs, that overpower the ones of understanding, there is who takes, with Him, rivals. The sūrah of the Table states the measure of the love of God: (3:31 — **“If you ˹sincerely˺ love Allah, then follow me”**). The verse is thus the other face of the verse before: the signs of the oneness overpower the ones of understanding, and the rivals are taken, by the ones who love them as the love of God — and the verse answers them with the love of the believers, that is stronger, and with the seeing of the wrongdoers, that would know, if it were.

---

## Verse 2:166

> ˹Consider the Day˺ when those who misled others will disown their followers—when they face the torment—and the bonds that united them will be cut off

**THE DISOWNING OF THAT DAY**

The verse carries the love of the rivals to its end. Al-Qurṭubī reads: when those who were followed disowned — He means the masters and the heads; they disowned the ones who followed them, upon the disbelief — from Qatādah, and ʿAṭāʾ, and al-Rabīʿ; and Qatādah and al-Suddī said: they are the devils, the ones who lead astray, who disowned the humans; and it is said: it is general, in everyone who is followed. And they saw the punishment — He means the followers and the followed, together. Al-Rabīʿ said: the heads disowned the followers, on the Day of Resurrection. Al-Ṭabarī gives the verse its verification, by the context: when the verse is, upon that, evidencing, then the interpretation of al-Suddī is sound, in His saying, and among the people are those who take rivals, of God — that the rivals, in this place, were only intended, the rivals, of the men, whom they obey, in what they commanded them of, of the affair, and they disobey God, in their obedience of them, as the believers obey God, and disobey other than Him — and the interpretation of the saying of the one who said: when those who were followed disowned — they are the devils, who disowned their allies, of the humans — is spoiled; because this verse is only, in the context of the report, of the ones who take the rivals.

**THE BONDS AND THEIR CUTTING**

Al-Qurṭubī reads the bonds by their root: and the bonds were cut off — meaning, the connections, by which they used to connect, in this world, of the womb, and other than it — from Mujāhid, and others; the one is a bond, and a connection; and the root of the bond is the rope, by which a thing is tied, and pulled; then everything by which a thing is drawn, was made a bond; and al-Suddī and Ibn Zayd said: the bonds are their deeds; and the bond is the direction. Mujāhid said: and the bonds were cut off — the affection; and in his report, through Ibn Jurayj: a connecting, that was between them, by affection, in this world; and Ibn ʿAbbās said the like of it. Qatādah reads: and the bonds were cut off — the bonds of the regret, on the Day of Resurrection, and the bonds of the connecting, that were between them, in this world, by which they used to connect, and by which they used to love one another, and they became, upon them, enmity, on the Day of Resurrection; then some of you will disbelieve in some, and some of you will curse some; and God said: the close friends, on that Day, are enemies to one another, except the righteous — so every closeness became enmity, upon its people, except the closeness of the righteous. Al-Rabīʿ said: the bonds — he says: the bonds are the regret; and Ibn ʿAbbās said, in another report: the bonds — he says: the dwellings were cut off from them. The sūrah of Ibrāhīm gives the disowning its tongue, from the one who called to the following: (14:22 — **“I cannot save you, nor can you save me”**). The verse is thus the answering of the love of the rivals: the love that was set up, against God, in this world, becomes the disowning of that Day — and the bonds that held the rivals to their followers are cut off, because the bonds were other than the bond of the righteous, that alone survives the cutting.

---

## Verse 2:167

> The ˹misled˺ followers will cry, “If only we could have a second chance, we would disown them as they disowned us.” And so Allah will make them remorseful of their misdeeds. And they will never ˹be able to˺ leave the Fire

**THE WISH OF THE FOLLOWERS**

The verse answers the disowning of the heads, with the wish of the followers. Al-Qurṭubī reads: and those who followed said: if only we had a return — the anna is in the place of the raising, meaning: if only it were established, that we had a returning; and we would disown them, from them, is the answer of the wishing; and the return is the returning, and the coming back, to a state that had been; meaning, the followers said: if only we were sent back, to this world, until we worked righteously, and disowned them, as they disowned us. Qatādah said: and those who followed said: if only we had a return — meaning, a returning, to this world; and al-Rabīʿ said: the followers said: if only we had a return, to this world, that we would disown them, from them, as they disowned us. The wish is thus the mirror of the disowning: the heads disowned, upon the seeing of the punishment; and the followers wish, to disown, upon the returning — and the returning is not given to them.

**THE REGRETS AND THEIR SHOWING**

Al-Ṭabarī reads the second clause of the verse: so God shows them their deeds, as regrets upon them; and some said: the meaning of that is: thus God shows them their deeds, that He obligated upon them, in this world, and they wasted them, and did not work by them — until the reward, that God had prepared for them, if they had worked by them, in their life, of the dwellings and the bounties, deserved to be theirs, other than them, by the obedience of his Lord; and what escaped them, of the reward, became sorrow, and regret, and heartache, upon them. Al-Suddī carries the verse its scene: he claimed that Paradise is raised up, for them, and they look upon it, and upon their dwellings therein, if they had obeyed God, and it is said to them: those are your dwellings, if you had obeyed God; then they are divided, between the believers, and they inherit them; and that is when they regret. Al-Rabīʿ said: thus God shows them their deeds, as regrets upon them — and their evil deeds became regrets, upon them, on the Day of Resurrection; and Ibn Zayd said: are they not their evil deeds, by which God made them enter the Fire? So He made them regrets, upon them; and He made the deeds of the people of Paradise, for them. Al-Ṭabarī gives the verse its verdict: what is worthier, of the interpretation of the verse, is what the apparent indicated, other than what the inner contained, that has no evidence, that it is intended by it; and what al-Suddī said, in that, is a going, that the verse bears, but it is a distant going — there is no report, by which the evidence stands, that it is as he mentioned, and no evidence, in the apparent of the verse, that it is intended by it; and when the affair was thus, the apparent of the recitation is not turned, to the inner of the interpretation. The sūrah of the Poets gives the wish its words, from the tongues of its people: (26:102 — **“If only we could have a second chance”**). The verse is thus the closing of the scene of the rivals: the disowning of the heads is answered by the wish of the followers, and the deeds are shown as regrets, and the fire is not departed from — and the three closings are the three endings, of the love that was set up, against the love of God.

---

## Verse 2:168

> O humanity! Eat from what is lawful and good on the earth and do not follow Satan’s footsteps. He is truly your sworn enemy

**THE ADDRESS TO ALL THE PEOPLE**

The verse turns, from the scene of the rivals, to the address of all the people. Al-Ṭabarī reads: God means, by that: O people, eat, of what I have made lawful for you, of the foods, upon the tongue of My messenger Muḥammad ﷺ, and made good for you, of what you forbid, to yourselves, of the baḥīra, and the sāʾiba, and the waṣīla, and what is like that, of what I did not forbid to you — other than what I forbade you, of the foods and the meals, and made unclean, of the carrion, and the blood, and the flesh of the swine, and what was slaughtered, upon it, for other than Me; and leave the footsteps of the devil — the one who destroys you, and ruins you, and brings you to the watering-places of the destruction, and forbids you your wealths — so do not follow them, and do not work by them. Al-Qurṭubī gives the verse its occasion, and its generality: it is said: it came down, concerning Thaqīf, and Khuzāʿah, and Banū Mudlij, in what they forbade, to themselves, of the cattle — and the wording is general; and the good, here, is the lawful, so it is an emphasis, by the difference of the wording — and this is the saying of Mālik, in the good; and al-Shāfiʿī said: the good is the pleasing to the taste, so it is a diversifying; and for that, the eating of the filthy animals is prevented. Al-Ṭabarī reads the two words by their roots: the meaning of His saying, lawful, is: free, and it is a verbal noun, from the saying of the sayer: this thing has become free, for you, meaning: it has become free, for you; and of the speech of the Arabs: it is free, for you, meaning: free.

**THE FOOTSTEPS AND THEIR FORBIDDING**

Al-Ṭabarī reads the forbidding of the footsteps: and the meaning, in the forbidding of following his footsteps, is the forbidding of his path, and his trace, in what he called to, of what is other than the obedience of God; and the people of interpretation differed, of the meaning of the footsteps: some said: the footsteps of the devil are his work; Ibn ʿAbbās said, in the report of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭalḥah: the footsteps of the devil — he says: his work; and Mujāhid said: his footsteps — his sin, and: his offences. Al-Qurṭubī gives the word its readings, and its verdict: Abū al-Sammāl al-ʿAdawī and ʿUbayd ibn ʿUmayr read, khaṭawāt, with the opening of the khāʾ and the ṭāʾ; and it was reported, from ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, and Qatādah, and al-Aʿraj, and ʿAmr ibn Maymūn, and al-Aʿmash, khuṭuʾāt; and the correct one is that the wording is general, in everything, other than the sunan and the legislations, of the innovations and the disobediences. The sūrah of the Table states the same eating, upon the believers: (5:88 — **“Eat of the good, lawful things provided to you by Allah”**). The verse is thus the opening of the halal, after the closing of the rivals: the people, all of them, are addressed with the eating, of the lawful and the good, and the forbidding, of the footsteps — and the two forbidings are joined, because the forbidding of the lawful, and the following of the devil, are the two workings, of the one enemy.

---

## Verse 2:169

> He only incites you to commit evil and indecency, and to claim against Allah what you do not know

**THE THREE COMMANDS OF THE ENEMY**

The verse unfolds the footsteps of the verse before, into their three commands. Al-Ṭabarī reads: God means, by His saying, he only commands you: the devil, with the evil, and the indecency, and that you say, upon God, what you do not know; and the evil is the sin, like the harm, from the saying of the sayer: this affair harmed you, harming, and it is what harms the doer; and as for the indecency, then it is a verbal noun, like the ease and the hardship, and it is everything whose mentioning is counted indecent, and whose hearing is ugly; and it was said: the evil, that God mentioned, is the disobediences of God — and if it is thus, then God named them evil, because they harm their doer, by the evil of their outcome, with Him; and it was said: the indecency is fornication — and if it is thus, then it is named thus, for the ugliness of its hearing, and the disliked of what its doer is remembered by — and al-Suddī said: he only commands you, with the evil, and the indecency: as for the evil, the disobedience; and as for the indecency, the fornication. Al-Qurṭubī gives the evil its name: the evil was named evil, because it harms its doer, by the evil of its outcomes; and it is the verbal noun of harmed him, harming, and causing harm, when it grieved him.

**THE THIRD COMMAND**

Al-Ṭabarī reads the third command by its occasion: and as for His saying, and that you say, upon God, what you do not know, then it is what they used to forbid, of the baḥīra, and the sāʾiba, and the waṣīla, and the ḥām, and claim that God forbade that; and God said, to them: God did not appoint a baḥīra, nor a sāʾiba, nor a waṣīla, nor a ḥām, but those who disbelieve fabricate a lie, upon God, and most of them do not reason; and He informed them, in this verse, that their saying: God forbade this! is of the lying, that the devil commands them with, and that He made it lawful, for them, and made it good, and did not forbid its eating. The three commands are thus read as one chain: the evil, that harms its doer; the indecency, whose hearing is ugly; and the saying upon God, without knowledge, that makes the forbidden lawful, and the lawful forbidden — and the third is the greatest of the three, because the first two are sins, of the self, and the third is a lie, upon the Lord. The sūrah of the Elevated Places states the same saying, in the answer of the prophets: (7:28 — **“How can you attribute to Allah what you do not know?”**). The verse is thus the unfolding of the enmity, of the verse before: the enemy is made plain, by the naming of his three commands — and the commands are named, so that the one who is commanded, knows the commander.

---

## Verse 2:170

> When it is said to them, “Follow what Allah has revealed,” they reply, “No! We ˹only˺ follow what we found our forefathers practicing.” ˹Would they still do so,˺ even if their forefathers had ˹absolutely˺ no understanding or guidance

**THE CALL AND THE ANSWER**

Ibn Isḥāq carries the verse its occasion, from Ibn ʿAbbās: the Messenger of God ﷺ called the Jews, of the People of the Book, to Islam, and made them desire it, and warned them, of the punishment of God, and His retribution; and Rāfiʿ ibn Khārijah, and Mālik ibn ʿAwf, said to him: rather, we follow what we found our fathers upon, for they were more knowing, and better, than us! And God sent down, concerning that, of their saying: and when it is said to them: follow what God has revealed, they say: rather, we follow what we found our fathers upon. Al-Ṭabarī gives the verse its reference, and its verdict: this verse has two aspects of interpretation: the one of them is that the hāʾ and the mīm, of His saying, and when it is said to them, return upon the who, of His saying, and among the people are those who take rivals, of God; and the other is that they are of the mentioning of the people; and the worthiest, of the interpretation of the verse, is that the hāʾ and the mīm, in His saying, to them, are of the mentioning of the people, and that it is a returning, from the address, to the report of the absent — because that is in the aftermath of His saying: O people, eat, of what is in the earth — and because it came down, concerning a people, of the Jews, who said that, when they were called to Islam. And the interpretation of His saying, follow what God has revealed, is: work, by what God revealed, in His Book, upon His messenger, and make lawful its lawful, and forbid its forbidden, and make it, for yourselves, an imām, by whom you are guided, and a leader, whose rulings you follow.

**THE FATHERS AND THEIR KNOWING**

Qatādah said: they say: rather, we follow what we found our fathers upon — meaning: what we found our fathers upon; and al-Rabīʿ said the like of it. The answer of the verse is not given to the fathers, but to the following: even if their fathers did not reason a thing, and were not guided — the following is made conditional, upon the knowing of the followed, and the fathers of the answer are the fathers, of whom it is not known, that they reasoned, or were guided. Al-Ṭabarī gives the conditional its force: the hamza, of His saying, even if their fathers, is the hamza of the denying, that enters upon the wāw — meaning: would they follow them, even if their fathers did not reason a thing, of the religion, and were not guided, to the truth? The sūrah of the Table states the same answer, in the same words: (5:104 — **“What we found our forefathers practicing is good enough for us.”**). The verse is thus the meeting of the two calls: the call of the revelation, that says follow what God has revealed, and the call of the fathers, that says we follow what we found our fathers upon — and the verse answers the second call, with the question of the knowing: for the following is not a virtue, by itself, but by the knowing of the followed; and the fathers, who are followed, without the knowing, are not followed, but carried.

---

## Verse 2:171

> The example of the disbelievers ˹not responding to the Messenger’s warning˺ is like a flock not comprehending the calls and cries of the shepherd. ˹They are wilfully˺ deaf, dumb and blind so they have no understanding

**THE PARABLE OF THE CALLING**

The verse gives the answer of the forefathers its parable. Al-Qurṭubī reads: God likened the admonisher of the disbelievers, and their caller — and he is Muḥammad ﷺ — to the shepherd who calls out to the sheep and the camels; and they hear nothing but his calling and his crying, and do not understand what he says; thus interpreted it Ibn ʿAbbās, and Mujāhid, and ʿIkrima, and al-Suddī, and al-Zajjāj, and al-Farrāʾ, and Sībawayhi; and this is the end of the conciseness. Sībawayhi gives the parable its precision: they were not likened to the caller, but to the called; and the poet said: call out to your sheep, O Jarīr, for only your own self has promised you, in the wilderness, a straying. Ibn ʿAbbās carries the parable its animals, in the report of ʿIkrima: he is like the sheep, and the like of that; and in his other report: like the camel, and the donkey, and the sheep — if you say to one of them: eat! it does not know what you say, other than that it hears your voice; and thus is the disbeliever: if you command him with a good, or forbid him from an evil, or admonish him, he does not understand what you say, other than that he hears your voice; and in the report of Ibn Jurayj: the parable of the beast, that is called, and hears, and does not understand what is said to it; thus is the disbeliever: he hears the voice, and does not understand.

**THE TWO FACES OF THE PARABLE**

Mujāhid said, in the report of Ibn Abī Najīḥ: a parable that God struck, of the disbeliever, who hears what is said to him, and does not understand, like the beast, that hears the calling, and does not understand; and in his other report: like the one who calls out — the shepherd — by that which hears not, of the beasts. Al-Suddī reads: it does not understand what is said to it, except that it is called, and comes, or cried to, and goes. Al-Rabīʿ said: it is the parable of the disbeliever, who hears the voice, and does not understand what is said to him. Al-Ṭabarī gives the verse its second face: it is possible that the meaning be, upon this interpretation: and the parable of those who disbelieve, in the little of their understanding, from God and from His Messenger, is the parable of the called, of the beasts, that does not understand, of the command and the prohibition, other than the voice; and that is, if it be said to it: fodder! bring the water! it does not know what is said to it, other than the voice that it hears, of its sayer; and thus the disbeliever — his parable, in the little of his understanding, of what he is commanded by, and forbidden from, by the evil of his reflecting upon it, and the little of his looking and his thinking, is the parable of this called one, in what it is commanded by, and forbidden from; and the meaning is, upon this, for the called, and the speech comes out, upon the caller. Al-Ṭabarī gives the verse its people: the evidence, of that, is what is before it, of the verses, and what is after it, then they are intended by it; and the verse that is before this verse came down, concerning them — the Jews — as is reported from Ibn ʿAbbās. The sūrah of the Congregation gives the same parable, of the carrying without the understanding: (62:5 — **“is that of a donkey carrying books”**). The verse is thus the parable of the calling, upon the two faces: the face of the caller, whose calling reaches the ears, and the face of the called, whose understanding stops at the voice — and the deafness of the verse is not of the ears, but of the understanding.

---

## Verse 2:172

> O believers! Eat from the good things We have provided for you. And give thanks to Allah if you ˹truly˺ worship Him ˹alone˺

**THE ADDRESS OF THE BELIEVERS**

The verse turns the eating, from all the people, to the believers. Al-Qurṭubī reads: this is an emphasis, of the first command, and He singled out the believers, here, with the mentioning, in preference; and what is intended, by the eating, is the benefiting, from all the aspects; and it is said: it is the customary eating. Al-Ṭabarī reads the verse by its words: God means, by His saying, O you who believe: O you who verified God, and His Messenger, and confessed, for God, the servitude, and submitted, to Him, the obedience — eat, of the goods, of what We provided you: He means: feed, of the lawful of the provision, that We made lawful for you, and made good, for you, by My making it lawful, for you, of what you used to forbid, and I had not forbidden it to you, of the foods and the drinks; and give thanks to God: He says: and praise God, with what He is deserving of, from you, for the bounties that He provided you, and made good, for you; if it is Him you worship: He says: if you are submitting to His command, hearing, obeying, then eat, of what He permitted you its eating, and made lawful, and made good, for you, and leave, in its forbidding, the footsteps of the devil. Al-Ḍaḥḥāk said of His saying, O you who believe: He says: they verified.

**THE THANKING AND ITS HADITH**

Al-Ṭabarī joins the verse to the verse before: and We have mentioned some of what they used, in their jāhiliyyah, to forbid, of the foods — and it is what He urged them to its eating, and forbade them the believing of its forbidding, since their forbidding of it, in the jāhiliyyah, was an obedience, from them, to the devil, and a following, of the people of the disbelief, of their fathers and their forefathers; then He made plain, for them, what He had forbidden, upon them, and detailed it, for them, explained. Qurṭubī carries the verse its ḥadīth: in the Ṣaḥīḥ of Muslim, from Abū Hurayrah, who said: the Messenger of God ﷺ said: O people, God is good, and does not accept, except the good; and God commanded the believers, with what He commanded the messengers; and he said: O messengers, eat of the goods, and work righteously, indeed I, of what you work, am knowing; and he said: O you who believe, eat, of the goods, of what We provided you; then he mentioned the man, who lengthens the journey, disheveled, dusty, stretching his two hands, to the heaven: O Lord! O Lord! — while his food is forbidden, and his drink is forbidden, and his clothing is forbidden, and he was nourished with the forbidden — how then is that one answered? The sūrah of the Believers gives the command of the messengers, that the ḥadīth recited: (23:51 — **“Eat from what is good and lawful, and act righteously”**). The verse is thus the second eating: the eating of all the people was the permission of the lawful, and the eating of the believers is the thanking of the Provider — and the ḥadīth joins the two commands, of the messengers and of the believers, in one eating, and makes the answering of the supplication stand, upon the goodness of the food.

---

## Verse 2:173

> He has only forbidden you ˹to eat˺ carrion, blood, swine, and what is slaughtered in the name of any other than Allah. But if someone is compelled by necessity—neither driven by desire nor exceeding immediate need—they will not be sinful. Surely Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful

**THE RESTRICTION OF THE FORBIDDING**

The verse gives the eating of the verse before its boundary. Al-Qurṭubī reads the verse by its restriction: in it are thirty-four questions: the first — His saying, indeed He forbade you the carrion — innamā is a word, placed for the restriction, containing the negation and the affirmation, and it affirms what the address reaches, and negates what is other than it; and it restricted, here, the forbidding — especially, as it came, in the aftermath of the making lawful, in His saying: O you who believe, eat, of the goods, of what We provided you — and it gave the permission, upon the absoluteness; then He followed it, with the mentioning of the forbidden, by the word of the restriction, innamā; and that comprehended the two sections, together; so there is no forbidden thing, that comes out, of this verse; and it is Medinan, and it was confirmed, by the other verse, that is reported to have come down, at ʿArafah: say, I do not find, in what is revealed to me, a forbidden thing, for the one who eats — and the making plain was complete, in the first and the last — this is the saying of Ibn al-ʿArabī. And the carrion is installed, by He forbade, and the mā is the restrainer; and Abū Jaʿfar read: it was forbidden, with the joining of the ḥāʾ, and the breaking of the rāʾ, and the raising of the names.

**THE FOUR AND THE NECESSITY**

Al-Ṭabarī carries the fourth of the four its faces: Mujāhid said, in the report of Ibn Abī Najīḥ: and what is offered, for other than God — what is slaughtered, for other than God; and Ibn ʿAbbās said, in the report of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭalḥah: and what is offered, for other than God — He means: what is offered, for the ṭawāghīt, all of them, meaning: what is slaughtered, for other than God, of the people of the disbelief, other than the Jews and the Christians; and al-Rabīʿ said: and what is offered, for other than God — He says: that upon which is mentioned, other than the name of God. And the verse gives the necessity its ruling: but whoever is compelled, not desiring, nor transgressing; Mujāhid said, in the report of Ibn Abī Najīḥ, twice: not desiring, nor transgressing — He says: not a cutter of the way, nor a parting from the imams, nor a going out, in the disobedience of God — for him is the concession; and whoever went out desiring, or transgressing, in the disobedience of God, there is no concession for him, even if he is compelled to it; and in the report of Jābir, from Mujāhid and ʿIkrima: not desiring — seeking it; nor transgressing — transgressing upon what keeps alive his self; and al-Rabīʿ said: from other than that he seeks the forbidden, and transgresses it — do you not see that He says: and whoever seeks beyond that, those are the transgressors? The sūrah of the Cattle states the same restriction, in the tongue of the Messenger: (6:145 — **“anything forbidden to eat except carrion”**). The verse is thus the closing of the eating: the permission was made absolute, by the addressing of the believers, and the forbidding was restricted, by the word of the restriction — and the necessity is the door, between the two, that opens the forbidden, for the compelled, and closes it, upon the transgressor.

---

## Verse 2:174

> Indeed, those who hide Allah’s revelations, trading them for a fleeting gain consume nothing but fire into their bellies. Allah will neither speak to them on the Day of Judgment, nor will He purify them. And they will suffer a painful punishment

**THE EATING OF THE FIRE**

The verse returns, from the forbidding of the foods, to the hiding of the Book. Al-Qurṭubī reads: those who hide what God sent down, of the Book — He means the scholars of the Jews, who hid what God sent down, in the Torah, of the description of Muḥammad ﷺ, and the soundness of his message; and the meaning of He sent down is: He made plain. Al-Ṭabarī reads the verse by its trade: God means, by His saying, those ones: these, who hide what God sent down, of the Book, in the affair of Muḥammad ﷺ, for the base of the bribe they are given, and distort, for that, the verses of God, and change their meanings — they eat not, in their bellies — by their eating, what they ate, of the bribes, upon that, and the wage, and what they took, for it, of the price — except the fire — meaning: except what brings them to the fire, and makes them enter it; as God said: those who eat the property of the orphans, unjustly, eat not, in their bellies, except the fire — and al-Rabīʿ said of His saying, they eat not, in their bellies, except the fire: He says: what they took, for it, of the wage.

**THE THREE DENIALS OF THAT DAY**

Al-Ṭabarī raises the question of the belly, and answers it: if one says: is there an eating, in other than the belly, that it be said: they eat not, in their bellies? It is said: the Arabs say: I hungered, in other than my belly, and I was sated, in other than my belly; and it was said: in their bellies, for that, as one says: so-and-so did this, himself. And He gives the verse its three denials: and God will not speak to them, on the Day of Resurrection — He says: He will not speak to them, with what they love, and desire; and as for what harms them, and they hate, then He will speak to them — because He informed that He says to them, when they say: our Lord, take us out of it, then if we return, then we are wrongdoers — He said: be driven away, into it, and do not speak to Me; and as for His saying, nor will He purify them, then He means: He will not purify them, from the dirt of their sins, and their disbelief; and theirs is a painful punishment — meaning: a painful one. The sūrah of the Women states the same eating, upon the same fire: (4:10 — **“consume nothing but fire into their bellies”**). The verse is thus the answer of the hiding, by the image of the eating: the hider eats the bribe, in this world, and the eating is named, by its outcome, the fire — and the three denials of that Day answer the three works of the hiding: the distorting of the verses, and the changing of the meanings, and the taking of the price.

---

## Verse 2:175

> They are the ones who trade guidance for misguidance and forgiveness for punishment. How persistent are they in pursuit of the Fire

**THE TRADE AND ITS TWO SIDES**

The verse reads the hiding as a trade. Al-Ṭabarī reads: God means, by His saying, those are the ones who purchased the straying, with the guidance: those who took the straying, and left the guidance, and took what obligates, for them, the punishment of God, on the Day of Resurrection, and left what obligates, for them, His forgiveness, and His pleasure; and He sufficed, by the mentioning of the punishment, and the forgiveness, from the mentioning of the cause, that obligates the two of them, for the understanding of its hearers, of its meaning, and what is intended, by it. The trade is thus read, by its two sides: the side of the taking — the straying, and the punishment — and the side of the leaving — the guidance, and the forgiveness — and the purchase is named, because the price was given, of the will, and the choice.

**THE WONDER OF THE PATIENCE**

Al-Ṭabarī carries the wonder of the verse its readings: Qatādah said of His saying, how patient they are, toward the fire: He says: how bold they are, upon the work, that brings them near, to the fire; and in the report of ʿAbd al-Razzāq, from Maʿmar, from Qatādah: how bold they are, upon it; and al-Ḥasan said: by God, they have not, toward it, of the patience — but rather, how bold they are, toward the fire; and it was reported, from Mujāhid, or Saʿīd ibn Jubayr, or some of his companions: how bold they are; and al-Rabīʿ said: how bold they are, and patient, toward the fire; and Mujāhid said, in the report of Ibn Abī Najīḥ: how active they are, in the falsehood. The wonder is thus not a wonder of God, of the patience, but a naming of their boldness, upon the work, that brings them near to the fire, by the name of the patience, that is its image — for the one who works the work, that brings him to the fire, and does not turn from it, is, in his persistence, like the patient, and in his persisting, the opposite of the patient. The sūrah of the Repentance states the other trade, of the other people: (9:111 — **“Allah has indeed purchased from the believers their lives and wealth in exchange for Paradise”**). The verse is thus the mirror of the trade: the hiders purchased the fire, with the guidance, and the believers were purchased, with the souls and the wealths, for the Paradise — and the two purchases are the two endings, of the one price, that every soul pays, whether it wills or not.

---

## Verse 2:176

> That is because Allah has revealed the Book in truth. And surely those who differ regarding it are totally engrossed in opposition

**THE CAUSE OF THE AFFAIR**

The verse gives the trade of the verse before its cause. Al-Qurṭubī reads: that — that is in the place of the raising, and it is a pointing, to the ruling, as if He said: that is the ruling, with the fire; and al-Zajjāj said: its estimate is: the affair is that, or that is the affair, or that is the punishment, for them; and al-Akhfash said: the predicate, of that, is suppressed. Al-Ṭabarī carries the verse its faces: some said: what is intended, by that, is their doing, this, that they do — of their boldness, upon the punishment of the fire, in their opposing the command of God, and their hiding, from the people, what God sent down, in His Book, and commanded them, with the making plain of, of the affair of Muḥammad ﷺ, and the affair of his religion — is because God sent down the Book, with the truth; and others said: the meaning of that is that God described the people of the fire, and said: how patient they are, toward the fire; then He said: this punishment, is by their disbelief — and that, here, is the one, that is permitted, in its place, that; as if He said: We did that, because God sent down the Book, with the truth, and they disbelieved in it.

**THE VERDICT OF THE POINTING**

Al-Ṭabarī gives the verse its verdict: the first, of the sayings, in the interpretation of the verse, is that God pointed, by His saying, that, to all of what was contained, by His saying: those who hide what God sent down, of the Book, until His saying: that is because God sent down the Book, with the truth, of His report, of the deeds of the ḥabrs of the Jews, and His mentioning, what He prepared, for them, of the punishment, upon that; and He said: this, that these ḥabrs, of the Jews, did — of their hiding, from the people, what they hid, of the affair of Muḥammad ﷺ, and his prophethood, with their knowledge of it, in seeking, from them, the accident of the base world — and by their opposing My command, and My obedience — and that — of My abandoning their purifying, and My not speaking to them — is because God sent down the Book, with the truth, and they disputed in it, and differed in it. And as for His saying, and those who differ, in the Book, are in a far schism — He means, by that, the Jews and the Christians; and al-Suddī said of His saying, and those who differ, in the Book, are in a far schism: they are the Jews and the Christians; he says: they are in a far enmity; they differed, in the Book of God, and the Jews disbelieved, in what God recounted, in it, of the account of ʿĪsā ibn Maryam, and his mother; and the Christians verified, with some of that, and disbelieved, with some of it; and they all disbelieved, in what God sent down, in it, of the command, with the verifying of Muḥammad ﷺ; and He said, to His Prophet Muḥammad ﷺ: these, who differed, in what I sent down, to you, O Muḥammad, are in a contending, and a parting, from the truth, far, from the rightness, and the hitting. The sūrah of Āl ʿImrān states the differing, upon its cause: (3:19 — **“out of mutual envy until knowledge came to them”**). The verse is thus the joining of the affair, of the hiding: the Book was sent down, with the truth, and the hiding was the opposing of the truth, and the differing was the far schism — and the three are the three standings, of the one Book, with its people.

---

## Verse 2:177

> Righteousness is not in turning your faces towards the east or the west. Rather, the righteous are those who believe in Allah, the Last Day, the angels, the Books, and the prophets; who give charity out of their cherished wealth to relatives, orphans, the poor, ˹needy˺ travellers, beggars, and for freeing captives; who establish prayer, pay alms-tax, and keep the pledges they make; and who are patient in times of suffering, adversity, and in ˹the heat of˺ battle. It is they who are true ˹in faith˺, and it is they who are mindful ˹of Allah˺

**THE QUESTION OF THE RIGHTEOUSNESS**

Qatādah carries the verse its occasion: it was mentioned to us, that a man asked the Prophet of God ﷺ, of the righteousness, and God sent down this verse; and it was mentioned to us, that the Prophet of God ﷺ called the man, and recited it, upon him; and the man used, before the obligations, when he witnessed that there is no god but God, and that Muḥammad is His servant and His Messenger, then died, upon that — it was hoped for him, and it was desired for him, of the good; and God sent down: righteousness is not, that you turn your faces, toward the east and the west. Al-Rabīʿ ibn Anas carries the verse its other occasion: the Jews used to pray, toward the west, and the Christians, toward the east, and it came down: righteousness is not, that you turn your faces, toward the east and the west. Ibn ʿAbbās carries the verse its place, in the report of Ibn Jurayj: this verse came down, in Madīnah: righteousness is not, that you turn your faces, toward the east and the west — He means: the prayer; He says: righteousness is not, that you pray, and do not work, other than that; and in his other report: He means: the prayer; He says: righteousness is not, that you pray, and do not work; and this is from when He was turned, from Makkah, to Madīnah, and the obligations came down, and the limits were limited; and God commanded the obligations, and the working, by them. Mujāhid said: righteousness is not, that you turn your faces, toward the east and the west — but rather, the righteousness is what was established, in the hearts, of the obedience of God; and he said, in the report of Ibn Jurayj: He means the prostration — but rather, the righteousness is what was established, in the heart, of the obedience of God.

**THE FIVE OF THE RIGHTEOUSNESS**

Al-Ṭabarī gives the verse its unfolding: the first of what God named, of the righteousness, is the belief in God, and the Last Day, and the angels, and the Book, and the prophets; then the giving of the wealth, upon its love, to the relatives, and the orphans, and the poor, and the son of the way, and the ones who ask, and in the freeing of the necks; then the establishing of the prayer, and the giving of the zakāh; then the fulfillers, of their covenant, when they covenant; then the patient, in the distress, and the harm, and the time of the fighting. Al-Shaʿbī carries the verse its ruling: Abū Ḥamzah asked al-Shaʿbī: when the man gives the zakāh of his wealth, is his Wealth made good, for him? And he recited this verse: righteousness is not, that you turn your faces, toward the east and the west, until He giving of the wealth, upon its love, until the end of it; then he said: Fāṭimah bint Qays told me, that she said: O Messenger of God, I have seventy mithqāls, of the gold; and the Prophet ﷺ said: in the wealth, there is a right, other than the zakāh; and he recited this verse: righteousness is not, until the end of the verse. Kathīr gives the six of the giving their names: the orphans are the children, who have none, to look after them; and the poor is the person, who does not have enough, of the food, and the clothing, or he has no dwelling; and the son of the way is the needy traveler, who runs out of his wealth, and is given, what helps him, to his land; and the guests are included, in this category; and Ibn ʿAbbās said: the son of the way is the guest. The sūrah of Āl ʿImrān states the measure of the giving, upon its love: (3:92 — **“You will never achieve righteousness until you donate some of what you cherish”**). The verse is thus the gathering of the righteousness, in the one verse: the five of the belief, and the six of the giving, and the three of the body, and the two of the soul — and the truthfulness of its ending, and the mindfulness of its ending, are the two namings, that the verse gives, to the ones who gathered it.

---

## Verse 2:178

> O believers! ˹The law of˺ retaliation is set for you in cases of murder—a free man for a free man, a slave for a slave, and a female for a female. But if the offender is pardoned by the victim’s guardian, then blood-money should be decided fairly and payment should be made courteously. This is a concession and a mercy from your Lord. But whoever transgresses after that will suffer a painful punishment

**THE WRITING OF THE RETALIATION**
