Al-Qurṭubī carries the verse its report: in it are seventeen questions: the first — al-Bukhārī, and al-Nasāʾī, and al-Dāraquṭnī reported, from Ibn ʿAbbās, who said: there was, among the Banū Isrāʾīl, the retaliation, and there was not, among them, the blood-money; and God said, to this community: the retaliation was written, upon you, in the slain, the free by the free, and the slave by the slave, and the female by the female; and whoever is pardoned, by his brother, a thing — and the pardon is the accepting, of the blood-money, in the intentional killing; then a following, by the good, and a paying, to him, by the good. Abū Mālik carries the verse its other occasion: there was, between two quarters, of the Anṣār, a fighting, and one of them had, over the other, the length, and it was as if they sought the excess; and the Prophet ﷺ came, to make peace, between them, and this verse came down: the free by the free, and the slave by the slave, and the female by the female; and the Prophet ﷺ made the free, by the free, and the slave, by the slave, and the female, by the female. Al-Qurṭubī gives the retaliation its name: the retaliation is taken, from the following of the trace, and it is its following; and from it is the storyteller, because he follows the traces, and the accounts; and the cutting of the hair is the following of its trace; and it is as if the killer traveled a way, of the killing, and the retaliated followed his trace, in it, and walked, upon his way, in that.

**THE PARDON AND THE TRANSGRESSION**

Al-Ṭabarī gives the verse its scope: when the differing was differed, in what this verse came down, of it, then the obligatory, upon us, is the using of it, in what it indicated, of the ruling, by the report, that cuts the excuse; and the reports were made manifest, from the Messenger of God ﷺ, by the general transmission: that the self of the free man is retaliation, by the self of the free woman; and it was evident, by that, that it was not intended, by His saying, the free by the free, and the slave by the slave, and the female by the female, that the slave is not retaliated, by the free, and that the female is not killed, by the male, and the male, by the female; and that the verse is intended, by it, one of the two other meanings: either that the retaliation is not transgressed, to other than the killer, and the offender, or that the verse came down, in a people, in particular, whom the Prophet ﷺ was commanded, that he make the blood-moneys of their slain, retaliation, some of them, of some. Mujāhid said: and whoever is pardoned, by his brother, a thing, then a following, by the good, and a paying, to him, by the good — and the pardon is the one, who pardons, of the blood, and takes the blood-money; and he said: and whoever transgresses, after that — and killed — for him is a punishment, a painful. Kathīr gives the transgression its people: after this, whoever transgresses the limits, he shall have a painful torment — means: the ones, who kill, in the retaliation, after the taking of the blood-money, or the accepting of it — they will suffer a painful and severe torment, from God; and the same was reported, from Ibn ʿAbbās, and Mujāhid, and ʿAṭāʾ, and ʿIkrimah, and al-Ḥasan, and Qatādah, and al-Rabīʿ ibn Anas, and al-Suddī, and Muqātil ibn Ḥayyān. The sūrah of the Table states the writing of the retaliation, upon the Banū Isrāʾīl: (5:45 — **“A life for a life”**). The verse is thus the writing of the retaliation, upon this community: the retaliation of the Banū Isrāʾīl, that had no blood-money, was joined to the pardon of this community, that has the blood-money — and the transgression, after the pardon, is answered, by the punishment, that is not of the retaliation.

---

## Verse 2:179

> There is ˹security of˺ life for you in ˹the law of˺ retaliation, O people of reason, so that you may become mindful ˹of Allah˺

**THE LIFE IN THE KILLING**

Al-Qurṭubī reads: and for you, in the retaliation, is a life — this is of the eloquent speech, the concise; and its meaning is: let not some of you kill some; it was reported by Sufyān, from al-Suddī, from Abū Mālik; and the meaning is: that the retaliation, when it is established, and the ruling, in it, is verified, then the one, who intends the killing of another, is deterred, by the fear, that he is retaliated, from him, and they lived, by that, both together. Mujāhid said, in the report of Ibn Abī Najīḥ: and for you, in the retaliation, is a life — He said: a deterrent, a restraining; and Ibn Jurayj said: a life, a prevention. Al-Rabīʿ carries the verse its working: God made this retaliation a life, and a lesson, for you; how many, of a man, have intended the calamity, and the fear, of the retaliation, prevented him, from the falling, in it! And God has restrained the servants, some of them, from some, by the retaliation.

**THE WORD AND ITS PREFERENCE**

Al-Alūsī gives the verse its place in the eloquence: it is a joining, upon His saying, it was written, upon you, and the intended, by it, is the settling of the self, upon the compliance, for the ruling of the retaliation, for its being harsh, upon the self; and it is speech, in the end of the eloquence; and the most concise, of the speech, among them, in this meaning, was: the killing is the most banishing, of the killing; and this word was preferred, over it, from faces: the first: the fewness of the letters, then the rest of the faces, until he said: the twelfth: its containing, what is fit, for the fighting, and it is the life, other than their saying, then it contains a negation, surrounded by two killings; the thirteenth: its being empty, of what their saying appears, of the being of the thing, a cause, for the negation of its self, and it is impossible. The verse is thus read, by the faces of its preferring: the life, that is named, in the place of the killing, that is intended — for the retaliation is not a killing, of the killing, but a preventing of the killing; and the ones of the understanding are addressed, because the understanding is what sees the life, in the place, where the eye sees the killing. The sūrah of the Table states the measure of the one life: (5:32 — **“it will be as if they killed all of humanity”**). The verse is the seal of the retaliation: the writing of the verse before was the ruling, and the life of this verse is the wisdom of the ruling — and the wisdom is named, to the ones of the understanding, because they are the ones, for whom the writing was written.

---

## Verse 2:180

> It is prescribed that when death approaches any of you—if they leave something of value—a will should be made in favour of parents and immediate family with fairness. ˹This is˺ an obligation on those who are mindful ˹of Allah˺

**THE WRITING OF THE WILL**

Al-Suddī carries the verse its place in the law: the will was written, upon you, when death approaches one of you, if he leaves wealth, the will, for the parents, and the relatives — as for the parents, and the relatives, then, on the day this verse came down, the people had no known inheritance; the man used to make a will, for his father, and his family, and it was divided, between them, until the Women abrogated it, and He said: God commands you, regarding your children. Shurayḥ carries the verse its practice, in the report of Qatādah: the man used to make a will, with his wealth, all of it, until the verse of the inheritance came down. Al-Ṭabarī reads the verse by its words: if he leaves wealth — Mujāhid said: wealth; and he said: the wealth, in the Qurʾān, all of it, is the wealth: for the love, of the wealth, is severe — the wealth; and: indeed I loved the love, of the wealth, from the remembrance, of my Lord — the wealth.

**THE ABROGATION AND ITS REMAINING**

Ibn ʿAbbās carries the verse its abrogation, upon the three reports: in the report of ʿIkrima: the will, for the parents, and the relatives — He said: it abrogated, of the one who inherits, and it did not abrogate, the relatives, who do not inherit; and in the report of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭalḥah: the will abrogated the parents, and it established the will, for the relatives, who do not inherit; and in his other report: the obligations, that are for the parents, and the relatives, abrogated the will; and he stood, and addressed the people, here, and recited, upon them, the sūrah of the Cow, to make plain, to them, from it, and he came, upon this verse: if he leaves wealth, the will, for the parents, and the relatives, and he said: this verse was abrogated. Mujāhid said: the inheritance was for the children, and the will was for the parents, and the relatives, and it is abrogated, abrogated it a verse, in the sūrah of the Women: God commands you, regarding your children. Al-Rabīʿ gives the abrogation its order: this was from before the Women was sent down; and when the verse of the inheritance came down, it abrogated the affair of the parents, and joined them, to the people of the inheritance, and the will became, for the people of the relatives, who do not inherit. Ṭāwūs carries the verse its guarding: whoever makes a will, for a people, and names them, and leaves the ones of his relatives, needy, it is taken, from them, and returned, to the ones of his relatives. ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib carries the verse its measure: he entered, upon a sick man, and the will was mentioned, to him, and he said: do not make a will; God only said: if he leaves wealth, and you have not left wealth; and in the report of Ibn Abī al-Zinād: then leave your wealth, for your sons. The sūrah of the Women states the inheritance, that abrogated the will, of the parents: (4:11 — **“Allah commands you regarding your children”**). The verse is thus the verse of the will, upon its two standings: the standing of its writing, when the people had no known inheritance, and the standing of its abrogation, when the inheritance was apportioned — and the will remains, upon the report of Ibn ʿAbbās, for the relatives, who do not inherit, because the right, that was written, is not lifted, but moved.

---

## Verse 2:181

> But whoever changes the will after hearing it, the blame will only be on those who made the change. Indeed, Allah is All-Hearing, All-Knowing

**THE SIN OF THE CHANGER**

The verse gives the will of the verse before its guard. Al-Ṭabarī reads the verse by its pronoun: the meaning of the speech is: the will was written upon you, when death approaches one of you, if he leaves wealth, the will, for the parents and the relatives, by the good, a right, upon the righteous; and we only said that the hāʾ, in His saying, whoever changes it, returns upon the suppressed, of the speech, that the apparent indicates, because His saying, the will was written upon you, when death approaches one of you, if he leaves wealth, the will, is of the saying of God, and that the changing, of the changer, is only for the will, of the one who wills; and as for the command, of God, with the will, then neither he, nor other than him, is able to change it, so it would be permitted that the hāʾ, in His saying, whoever changes it, return upon the will. Mujāhid said, in the report of Ibn Abī Najīḥ, twice: whoever changes it, after he has heard it — He said: the will.

**THE TWO FREEDOMS**

Qatādah said, in the report of ʿAbd al-Razzāq, from Maʿmar: whoever changes the will, after he has heard it — whoever changes the will, after he has heard it, then the sin, of what he changed, is upon him. Al-Suddī gives the verse its measure: whoever changes the will, that he willed by, and it was, by the good, then its sin is only, upon the one who changes it; he has wronged. ʿAṭāʾ ibn Abī Rabāḥ said of His saying, whoever changes it, after he has heard it, then its sin is only upon the ones who change it: it is executed, as he said. Ibn ʿAbbās gives the verse its two freedoms, in the report of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭalḥah: and the wage, of the one who willed, has fallen, upon God, and he is free, of its sin; and if he willed, in harm, his will is not permitted, as God said: without harm. The verse is thus read, by its two freedoms: the freedom of the one who willed — whose wage has fallen, upon God, and whose sin is lifted, from him, by the falling — and the freedom of the one who changes — whose sin is only upon himself, because the changing is his work, and not the work of the one who willed; and the harm of the will is answered, by the not permitting of it, because the will is the right of the nearness, and the harm is the opposing of the right. The sūrah of the Women states the guard of the will, in the apportioning of the inheritance: (4:12 — **“without harm”**).

---

## Verse 2:182

> Whoever suspects an error or an injustice in the will and brings about a ˹fair˺ settlement among the parties will not be sinful. Surely Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful

**THE REFORMING OF THE WILL**

The verse gives the changing of the verse before its other face. Mujāhid carries the verse its scene, in the report of Ibn Abī Najīḥ: whoever fears, from a willer, an error, or a sin, and reforms, between them, there is no sin, upon him — He said: this is when the man is attended, and he is dying; and if he is extravagant, they command him, with the justice, and if he falls short, they say: do thus, and give so-and-so, thus; and in his other report: this is when the man is attended, and he is in the death; and if he overlooks the injustice, they command him, with the justice, and if he falls short, of a right, they say: do thus, and give so-and-so, thus. Ibn ʿAbbās said, in the report of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭalḥah: whoever fears, from a willer, an error — meaning: a sin — He says: if the dead errs, in his will, or does wrong, in it, then there is no straitness, upon the guardians, that they return his error, to the right. Al-Rabīʿ said: whoever wills, with a will, of the injustice, and the executor returns it, to the truth, after his death, there is no sin, upon him — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said, in his report: and he reforms, between them — He says: the executor returned it, to the truth, after his death, and there is no sin, upon him.

**THE ERROR AND THE INTENT**

Al-Suddī gives the two words their names: whoever fears, from a willer, an error, or a sin, and reforms, between them, there is no sin, upon him — as for an error: then the error, in his will; and as for a sin: then the intent, that he intends, in his will, the wrong; and this one is greater, for his wage, that he not execute it, but that he reform, between them, upon what he sees, that it is the truth, and diminish some, and increase some; and he said: and this verse came down, in the parents, and the relatives. Ibn ʿAbbās said of His saying, an error: He means, by the error: the mistake; and Mujāhid said: an error, or a sin — He said: a mistake, or an intent. Ṭāwūs carries the verse its example: he is the man, who wills, for the children, of his daughter. The verse is thus read, by its two standings: the standing of the one who wills, whose error and intent are feared, and the standing of the one who reforms, who returns the error, to the right — and the reforming is not the changing, that is forbidden, of the verse before, because the changing is the opposing of the will, and the reforming is the returning of it, to the good, that the will was commanded, with. The sūrah of the Women states the reforming, between the two spouses, upon the same promise: (4:35 — **“If they desire reconciliation, Allah will restore harmony between them”**).

---

## Verse 2:183

> O believers! Fasting is prescribed for you—as it was for those before you—so perhaps you will become mindful ˹of Allah˺

**THE WRITING OF THE FAST**

The verse opens the passage of the fast. Al-Ṭabarī reads: and He means, by His saying, the fast was written upon you: the fast was obligated upon you; and His saying, as it was written upon those before you, means: it was obligated, upon those before you; then the people of interpretation differed, of the ones whom God intended, by His saying, as it was written upon those before you, and of the meaning, that the likening fell, in it, between the obligation of our fast, and the fast of the ones who were before us. Al-Shaʿbī carries the verse its history: were I to fast the year, all of it, I would break the fast, the day that is doubted in, and it is said: of Shaʿbān, and it is said: of Ramaḍān; and that is because the Christians were obligated, the month of Ramaḍān, as we were obligated; and they turned it, to the season; and that is because they were, perhaps, fasting it, in the heat, counting thirty days; then there came, after them, a generation, and they took, for themselves, the trust, and they fasted, before the thirty, a day, and after it, a day; then the later did not cease to be sunna-ed, with the sunnah of the generation, that was before it, until it became, to fifty.

**THE ONES BEFORE AND THE LIKENING**

Al-Suddī carries the verse its likening: O you who believe, the fast was written upon you, as it was written upon those before you — as for those who were before us: then the Christians; Ramaḍān was written upon them, and it was written upon them, that they not eat, and not drink, after the sleep, and that they not marry the women, the month of Ramaḍān; and the fasting of Ramaḍān was harsh, upon the Christians, and it was turned, upon them, in the winter and the summer; and when they saw that, they gathered, and made a fasting, in the season between the winter and the summer, and they said: we increase twenty days, by which we atone, for what we did! Al-Rabīʿ said: the fasting was written upon them, from the nightfall to the nightfall; and Mujāhid said: the People of the Book. Al-Ṭabarī gives the verse its verdict: O you who believe, the fast was obligated upon you, as it was obligated, upon those before you, of the People of the Book, counted days — and it is the month of Ramaḍān, all of it; because whoever was, after Ibrāhīm, was commanded, with the following of Ibrāhīm; and that is because God made him, for the people, an imām, and He informed us that his religion was the ḥanīfiyyah, the submitted; and He commanded our Prophet ﷺ, with the like of what He commanded, by it, whoever was before him, of the prophets; and the interpretation of His saying, so perhaps you will be mindful: He means, by it: that you may be mindful, of the eating of the food, and the drinking of the drink, and the intercourse of the women, in it; He says: I obligated upon you the fasting, and the withholding, from what you become, by the abandoning of the withholding from it, breaking — that you may be mindful, of what breaks you, in the time of your fasting. The sūrah of Maryam gives the fast its other form, of the vow: (19:26 — **“I have vowed silence”**). The verse is thus the writing of the fast, upon the three standings: the writing, that joins it to the ones before, and the likening, that makes it the one religion, and the mindfulness, that is its fruit.

---

## Verse 2:184

> ˹Fast a˺ prescribed number of days. But whoever of you is ill or on a journey, then ˹let them fast˺ an equal number of days ˹after Ramaḍân˺. For those who can only fast with extreme difficulty, compensation can be made by feeding a needy person ˹for every day not fasted˺. But whoever volunteers to give more, it is better for them. And to fast is better for you, if only you knew

**THE CHOICE OF THE FIRST FASTING**

ʿAmr ibn Murrah carries the verse its first standing: our companions reported that the Messenger of God ﷺ, when he came upon them, commanded them, with the fasting of three days, of every month, voluntarily, other than an obligation; then the fasting of Ramaḍān came down; and they were a people, who were not accustomed to the fasting, and the fasting was harsh, upon them; and whoever did not fast, fed a poor person; then this verse came down: whoever of you witnesses the month, let him fast it; and whoever is sick, or upon a journey, then a number, of other days; and the concession became, for the sick and the traveler, and we were commanded, with the fasting. Muʿādh ibn Jabal, and Ibn ʿUmar, and Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ carry the verse its practice: they were, after the coming down of this verse, in the time of the Messenger of God ﷺ, in the fasting of the month of Ramaḍān, at the choice, between its fasting, and the falling of the fidyah, from them, and between the breaking, and the ransoming, of their breaking, with the feeding of a poor person, for every day; and they were doing that, until whoever of you witnesses the month, let him fast it came down, and they were bound, with the obligation of its fasting, and the choice and the fidyah were voided.

**THE FIDYAH AND ITS STANDING**

Ibn ʿUmar gives the verse its abrogation: this verse was abrogated — meaning: and upon those who bear it, a fidyah, the feeding of a poor person — by the one after it: whoever of you witnesses the month, let him fast it, and whoever is sick, or upon a journey, then a number, of other days. And others said, of the ones who read: and upon those who bear it: that is not abrogated, and not a thing, of it, and it is a ruling, established, from when this verse came down, to the standing of the Hour; and they said: its interpretation is only: and upon those who bear it — in the state of their youth, and their newness, and in the state of their health, and their strength — if they become sick, and grow old, and are unable, from the old age, to fast, a fidyah, the feeding of a poor person — not that the people were conceded the breaking — while they were, upon the fasting, able — if they ransomed. And others read: and upon those who are made to bear it, a fidyah, the feeding of a poor person, and they said: it is the old man, and the old woman, who have grown old, from the fasting, and they are tasked, with the fasting, and do not bear it, and for them is that they break, and feed, in the place of every day they broke, a poor person; and they said: the verse is established, of the ruling, from when it came down, not abrogated, and they denied the saying of the one who said: it is abrogated. Ibn ʿAbbās used to read: they are made to bear it, and he used to say: it is standing, for the people, today; and ʿĀʾishah used to read: they are made to bear it. The sūrah of the Mutual Disillusion states the measure of the bearing: (64:16 — **“So be mindful of Allah to the best of your ability”**). The verse is thus the verse of the choice, and its lifting: the fasting was given, at the first, by the choice, and the fidyah, because the people were not accustomed to the fasting — and the choice was lifted, when the fasting became the custom, and the fidyah remained, upon the ones who bear it not, because the obligation does not fall, upon the unable.

---

## Verse 2:185

> Ramaḍân is the month in which the Quran was revealed as a guide for humanity with clear proofs of guidance and the standard ˹to distinguish between right and wrong˺. So whoever is present this month, let them fast. But whoever is ill or on a journey, then ˹let them fast˺ an equal number of days ˹after Ramaḍân˺. Allah intends ease for you, not hardship, so that you may complete the prescribed period and proclaim the greatness of Allah for guiding you, and perhaps you will be grateful

**THE MONTH AND ITS SENT-DOWNS**

Wāthilah carries the month its sent-downs, in the report of Qatādah, from Abū al-Mulayḥ: the scrolls of Ibrāhīm came down, the first night, of the month of Ramaḍān; and the Torah came down, for six, that had passed, of Ramaḍān; and the Gospel came down, for thirteen, that had passed; and the Qurʾān came down, for the twenty-fourth, of Ramaḍān. Al-Qurṭubī gives the month its beginning: the people of the history said: the first, of the ones who fasted Ramaḍān, was Nūḥ, when he came out, of the ship; and the saying of Mujāhid has preceded: God wrote Ramaḍān, upon every community. The verse is thus read, by the sent-downs, that came down, in it: the four books came down, in Ramaḍān, and the Qurʾān is the last of the four, and the one that fasts the month fasts the month, of the sent-downs.

**THE WITNESSING AND THE EASE**

ʿUbaydah carries the witnessing its ruling, in the report of Abū al-Bakhtarī: we were, with ʿUbaydah, and he recited this verse: whoever of you witnesses the month, let him fast it, and he said: whoever fasts a thing, of it, in the city, then let him fast the rest of it, if he goes out; and Ibn ʿAbbās used to say: if he wishes, he fasts, and if he wishes, he breaks. Al-Ṭabarī raises the question of the mind, and answers it: this interpretation has no meaning, because the insanity, if it drops, from the one who is with it, the obligation of the fasting, from the aspect of the loss, of its owner, the mind, all of the month, then it must be the way, of everyone who loses his mind, all of the month of the fasting; and all have agreed, that whoever loses his mind, all of the month of the fasting, by a fainting, or a delirium, then recovers, after the passing of the month, that upon him is the making up, of the month, all of it; and no one opposed that, whose opposing is permitted, upon the community; and when it is a consensus, then the obligatory is that the way, of everyone who was absent of mind, all of the month of the fasting, be the way of the fainted one. Mujāhid carries the journey its ease: by God, there is not, of the two of them, except a lawful thing, the fasting and the breaking, and God did not intend, by the breaking, except the easing, for His servants; and Ḥamzah al-Aslamī, the companion of the Messenger of God ﷺ, said: O Messenger of God, I consecrate the fasting — do I fast, in the journey? And the Messenger of God ﷺ said: it is only a concession, from God, for His servants; and whoever does it, then good and beautiful, and whoever leaves it, then there is no sin, upon him; and Ibn ʿAbbās was asked, of the fasting, in the journey, and he said: an ease, and a hardship — so take, by the ease of God. The sūrah of the Night of Glory states the sent-down, of the Qurʾān, in the month: (97:1 — **“sent this ˹Quran˺ down on the Night of Glory”**). The verse is thus the naming of the month, with the Qurʾān: the guidance, and the clear signs, and the criterion, are the three namings, of the sent-down — and the ease, that God intends, is the other naming, of the fasting, so that the month is gathered, between the sent-down of the Book, and the ease of the obligation.

---

## Verse 2:186

> When My servants ask you ˹O Prophet˺ about Me: I am truly near. I respond to one’s prayer when they call upon Me. So let them respond ˹with obedience˺ to Me and believe in Me, perhaps they will be guided ˹to the Right Way˺

**THE QUESTION OF THE NEARNESS**

Al-Ṭabarī carries the verse its occasions: the people differed, in what this verse was sent down, of it; and some said: it was sent down, concerning a sayer, who asked the Prophet ﷺ, and said: O Muḥammad, is our Lord near, that we whisper to Him, or far, that we cry to Him? And God sent down: and when My servants ask you, of Me, then I am near, I answer — the verse. Al-Ḥasan said: the companions of the Prophet ﷺ asked the Prophet ﷺ: where is our Lord? And God sent down: and when My servants ask you, of Me, then I am near, I answer the call of the caller, when he calls Me — the verse. And others said: rather, it was sent down, an answer, to the question of a people, who asked the Prophet ﷺ: in what hour do we call God?

**THE HOUR OF THE CALLING**

ʿAṭāʾ carries the verse its occasion, upon the three reports: when your Lord said: call Me, I will answer you came down, they said: in what hour? And he said: it came down: and when My servants ask you, of Me, then I am near, until his saying, perhaps they are guided; and in his other report: they said: if we knew, what hour we call! And it came down: and when My servants ask you, of Me, then I am near — the verse; and in the third: it reached him, that when your Lord said: call Me, I will answer you came down, the people said: if we knew, what hour we call! And it came down. Mujāhid said: call Me, I will answer you — they said: toward where? And it came down: wherever you turn, there is the face of God, indeed God is encompassing, knowing. The verse is thus read, by its two questions: the question of the nearness — is our Lord near, that we whisper to Him, or far, that we cry to Him? — and the question of the hour — if we knew, what hour we call! — and the answer of the two is one: I am near, I answer the call of the caller, when he calls Me; for the nearness makes the whispering and the crying one, and the answering makes the hours all one. The sūrah of the Forgiver states the command of the calling, that the people asked, of its hour: (40:60 — **“Call upon Me, I will respond to you”**). The verse is thus the verse of the nearness, between the verses of the fasting: the fasting is the withholding of the body, and the calling is the speaking of the heart — and the verse answers the caller, with the nearness, before he finishes his calling.

---

## Verse 2:187

> It has been made permissible for you to be intimate with your wives during the nights preceding the fast. Your spouses are a garment for you as you are for them. Allah knows that you were deceiving yourselves. So He has accepted your repentance and pardoned you. So now you may be intimate with them and seek what Allah has prescribed for you. ˹You may˺ eat and drink until you see the light of dawn breaking the darkness of night, then complete the fast until nightfall. Do not be intimate with your spouses while you are meditating in the mosques. These are the limits set by Allah, so do not exceed them. This is how Allah makes His revelations clear to people, so they may become mindful ˹of Him˺

**THE EASING OF THE NIGHT**

Al-Barāʾ carries the verse its occasion: they used, when they fasted, and one of them slept, not to eat a thing, until it was, of the morrow; and a man came, of the Anṣār, and he had worked, in his land, and he was exhausted, and wearied, and his eye overcame him, and he slept, and he became, of the morrow, spent; and this verse came down: and eat and drink, until the white thread becomes clear, to you, from the black thread, of the dawn. And in his other report: the companions of Muḥammad ﷺ used, when the man was fasting, and he slept, before he broke, not to eat, until its like; and Qays ibn Ṣirmah al-Anṣārī was fasting, and he had turned, that day, and worked, in his land; and when the breaking attended, he came to his wife, and said: do you have food? She said: no, but I will go, and seek, for you. ʿIkrimah joins the verse its other occasion: ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said to his wife: do not sleep, until I return, from the presence of the Messenger of God ﷺ; and she slept, before he returned, and he said to her: you are not sleeping! Then he struck her, until he came to the Prophet ﷺ, and mentioned that, to him, and this verse came down; and ʿIkrimah said: and eat and drink came down, concerning Abū Qays ibn Ṣirmah, of Banū al-Khazraj, who ate, after the sleep.

**THE GARMENT AND THE KINYAH**

Ibn ʿAbbās gives the verse its word, in the report of Bakr ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Muzanī: the rafath is the intercourse, but God is generous, He makes a kunyah; and in his other report: the touching is the intercourse, but God is generous, He makes a kunyah; and Mujāhid said: permitted to you, the night of the fasting, the rafath, to your women — He said: the intercourse. Zirr ibn Ḥubaysh carries the verse its practice: I became, one day, and I went, in the morning, to the mosque, and I said: if I passed, by the door of Ḥudhayfah! And it was opened, for me, and I entered, and there he was, having food warmed, for him, and he said: sit, until you eat; and I said: I intend the fasting; and he brought near his food, and he ate, and I ate, with him; then he stood, to a milch-camel, in the courtyard, and he took, to milk, from a side, and I milked, from a side, and he handed me, and I said: do you not see the dawn? And he said: drink! The sūrah of the Romans states the making of the spouses, upon the comfort: (30:21 — **“He created for you spouses from among yourselves so that you may find comfort in them”**). The verse is thus the easing of the night, after the night of the strictness: the strictness was of the custom of the first fasting, that held the sleep as a beginning of the day — and the verse returned the night to its ease, and made the spouses a garment, and made the dawn the boundary, between the two threads.

---

## Verse 2:188

> Do not consume one another’s wealth unjustly, nor deliberately bribe authorities in order to devour a portion of others’ property, knowing that it is a sin

**THE EATING OF THE WEALTH**

The verse turns, from the fasting, to the wealth. Al-Ṭabarī reads: and His saying, with the sin, means: with the forbidden, that God forbade, upon you; and you know — meaning: and you intend the eating of that, with the sin, upon an intending, from you, to what God forbade, upon you, of it, and a knowing, that your doing of that is a disobedience, to God, and a sin. Ibn ʿAbbās carries the verse its scene, in the report of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭalḥah: and do not eat your wealth, between you, with the falsehood, and offer it, to the judges — this is, of the man, upon whom is wealth, and there is not, upon him, in it, an evidence, and he denies the wealth, and disputes, in it, to the judges, while he knows that the right is upon him, and he knows that he is a sinner: an eater, of a forbidden thing. Mujāhid said, in the report of Ibn Abī Najīḥ, twice: and offer it, to the judges — He said: do not dispute, while you are a wrongdoer.

**THE JUDGE AND THE JUDGMENT**

Qatādah gives the verse its counsel: and do not eat your wealth, between you, with the falsehood, and offer it, to the judges — and it used to be said: whoever walks, with his adversary, while he is a wrongdoer, to him, then he is sinful, until he returns, to the truth; and know, O son of Ādam, that the judging, of the judge, does not make lawful, for you, a forbidden thing, and does not make right, for you, a falsehood; and the judge only judges, by the like of what he sees, and the witnesses bear witness, by it, and the judge is a man, he errs, and he hits; and know, that whoever has been judged, for him, with the falsehood, then his dispute is not cut off, until God gathers, between them, on the Day of Resurrection, and He judges, upon the one of the falsehood, for the one of the truth; and in the report of Maʿmar: do not offer the wealth, of your brother, to the judge, while you know that you are a wrongdoer, for his judging does not make lawful, for you, a thing, that was forbidden, upon you. Ibn Zayd reads: and do not eat your wealth, between you, with the falsehood, and offer it, to the judges — He says: he is more eloquent, than him, and more knowing, with the proof, and he disputes him, in his wealth, with the falsehood, that he may eat his wealth, with the falsehood; and he recited: O you who believe, do not eat your wealth, between you, with the falsehood, except that it be a trade, of a mutual content, from you; and he said: this is the gambling, that the people of the jāhiliyyah used to work, by it. Al-Ṭabarī gives the verse its grammar: one of the two faces is that His saying, and offer it, be a jussive, a joining, upon His saying, and do not eat your wealth, between you, with the falsehood — meaning: and do not offer it, to the judges; and it is mentioned that that is thus, in the reading of Ubayy, by the repeating of the particle of the prohibiting. The sūrah of the Women states the same prohibiting, upon the same wealth: (4:29 — **“Do not devour one another’s wealth illegally”**). The verse is thus the guarding of the wealth, by the two sides: the side of the eating, that is forbidden, by the falsehood, and the side of the offering, to the judges — and the judging of the judge does not change the right, because the judgment is upon the seen, and the right is upon the real.

---

## Verse 2:189

> They ask you ˹O Prophet˺ about the phases of the moon. Say, “They are a means for people to determine time and pilgrimage.” Righteousness is not in entering your houses from the back doors. Rather, righteousness is to be mindful ˹of Allah˺. So enter your homes through their ˹proper˺ doors, and be mindful of Allah so you may be successful

**THE QUESTION OF THE CRESCENTS**

Qatādah carries the verse its question: they asked the Prophet of God ﷺ, of that: why were these crescents made? And God sent down, in it, what you hear: they are times, for the people; and He made them, for the fasting of the Muslims, and their breaking, and for their rites, and their ḥajj, and for the waiting of their women, and the falling due of their debts, in things; and God is more knowing, of what puts right His creation. Al-Rabīʿ said: it was mentioned to us, that they said, to the Prophet ﷺ: why were the crescents created? And God sent down: they ask you, of the crescents, say, they are times, for the people, and the ḥajj; God made them times, for the fasting of the Muslims, and their breaking, and for their ḥajj, and their rites, and the waiting of their women, and the falling due of their debts. Al-Suddī said: they are the times of the divorcing, and the menstruation, and the ḥajj; and al-Ḍaḥḥāk said: He means: the falling due of their debts, and the time of their ḥajj, and the waiting of their women. Ibn ʿAbbās carries the verse its answer: the people asked the Messenger of God ﷺ, of the crescents, and this verse came down: they ask you, of the crescents, say, they are times, for the people, and the ḥajj — they know, by them, the falling due of their debts, and the waiting of their women, and the time of their ḥajj.

**THE DOORS AND THE BACKS**

Al-Barāʾ carries the verse its other question: they used, in the jāhiliyyah, when they entered the iḥrām, to come to the houses, from their backs, and not to come, from their doors; and it came down: and righteousness is not, that you come to the houses, from their backs — the verse. Mujāhid said: the associators used, when the man, of them, entered the iḥrām, to pierce a window, in the back of his house, and make it a ladder, and make his entering, from it; and the Messenger of God ﷺ came, one day, and with him a man, of the associators, and he came to the door, to enter, and entered, from it, and the man went, to enter, from the window, and the Messenger of God ﷺ said: what is your affair? And he said: I am aḥmas! And the Messenger of God ﷺ said: and I am aḥmas. Al-Suddī said: a people, of the Arabs, used, when they performed the ḥajj, not to enter their houses, from their doors; they used to pierce, in their backs; and when the Messenger of God ﷺ performed the ḥajj, of the farewell ḥajj, he came, walking, and with him a man, of those ones, and he was a Muslim; and when the Messenger of God ﷺ reached the door, of the House, the man held back, behind him, and refused, to enter, and he said: O Messenger of God, I am aḥmas! — meaning: I am in the iḥrām — and those ones, who used to do that, were called the ḥums. Al-Qurṭubī gives the crescents their wisdom: it is the making plain, of the face of the wisdom, in the increasing of the moon, and its diminishing, and it is the removing of the difficulty, in the terms, and the dealings, and the ḥajj, and the counting, and the fasting, and the breaking, and the term of the pregnancy, and the hirings. The sūrah of Yūnus states the making of the phases, upon the counting: (10:5 — **“the moon a reflected light, with precisely ordained phases”**). The verse is thus the joining of the two questions: the question of the crescents, that was answered, by the times, and the question of the backs, that was answered, by the doors — and the two answers are one righteousness: the entering of the affair, from its face.

---

## Verse 2:190

> Fight in the cause of Allah ˹only˺ against those who wage war against you, but do not exceed the limits. Allah does not like transgressors

**THE FIRST VERSE OF THE FIGHTING**

Al-Qurṭubī gives the verse its standing: this verse is the first verse, that came down, in the commanding, of the fighting; and there is no disagreement, that the fighting was forbidden, before the emigration, by His saying: repel, with what is better; and: pardon them, and turn aside; and: forsake them, a beautiful forsaking; and: you are not, over them, a controller; and what was the like of it, of what came down, in Makkah; and when he emigrated, to Madīnah, he was commanded, with the fighting, and it came down: and fight, in the way of God, the ones who fight you — the saying of al-Rabīʿ ibn Anas, and others; and it is reported, from Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq, that the first verse, that came down, in the fighting, is: permission was given, to the ones who are fought, because they were wronged; and the first is more. Al-Rabīʿ said: and fight, in the way of God, the ones who fight you, and do not transgress — this is the first verse, that came down, in the fighting; and when it came down, the Messenger of God ﷺ used to fight the one who fought him, and withhold, from the one who withheld, from him, until Barāʾah came down. Mujāhid said: and fight, in the way of God, the ones who fight you — for the companions of Muḥammad ﷺ; they were commanded, with the fighting of the disbelievers.

**THE ABROGATION AND THE UNSHAKEN**

Al-Qurṭubī carries the verse its history: the verse of the permission came down only, in the fighting, generally, for the one who fought, and for the one who did not fight, of the associators; and that is because the Prophet ﷺ went out, with his companions, to Makkah, for the ʿumrah; and when he stopped, at the Ḥudaybiyyah, near Makkah, the associators barred him, from the House, and he stayed, at the Ḥudaybiyyah, a month, and they reconciled him, upon that he return, of his year, that, as he came, upon that Makkah be emptied, for him, in the coming year, three days, and they reconciled him, upon that there be not, between them, a fighting, ten years; and he returned, to Madīnah; and when it was, of the next year, he prepared, for the ʿumrah of the making up, and the Muslims feared the treachery, of the disbelievers, and hated the fighting, in the sanctuary, and in the sacred month, and this verse came down — meaning: the fighting is lawful, for you, if the disbelievers fight you; and the verse is joined, to what preceded, of the mentioning of the ḥajj, and the coming to the houses, from their backs; and he ﷺ used to fight the one who fought him, and withhold, from the one who withheld, from him, until slay the associators came down, and this verse was abrogated — the saying of a group, of the scholars; and Ibn Zayd and al-Rabīʿ said: and fight the associators, all together, abrogated it, and he was commanded, with the fighting, for all of the disbelievers; and Ibn ʿAbbās, and ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz, and Mujāhid said: it is unshaken — meaning: fight the ones, who are, with the state, of the ones who fight you, and do not transgress, in the killing of the women, and the children, and the monks, and the like of them. The sūrah of the Ḥajj states the permission, of the fighting, upon the wronging: (22:39 — **“granted to those being fought, for they have been wronged”**). The verse is thus the opening of the fighting, upon its three standings: the standing of the forbidding, that was before the emigration, and the standing of the permission, that was for the ones who fought, and the standing of the unshaken, that holds the not transgressing — and the not transgressing is the verse that does not go, by the going of the abrogation, because the fighting is in the way of God, and the transgression is against the way.
